The arms of this Savitṛ, spread out for giving, extensive and golden, have spread covering the end parts of the firmament. Now the famous greatness of such a Savitṛ is praised. May Sūrya also grant to this Savitṛ the wish for action.

##### English translation
May the outspread, vast, and golden arms of Savitri extend to the ends of the sky : verily his greatness is glorified (by us): may the sun impart energy unto him.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स घा॑ नो दे॒वः स॑वि॒ता स॒हावा सा॑विष॒द्वसु॑पति॒र्वसू॑नि ।

### Printed p. 265

> विश्रयमाणो अमतिमुरूचीं मर्तभोजनमध रासते नः ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । घ । नः । देवः । सविता । सहऽवा । आ । साविषत् । वसुपतिः । वसूनि ।  
> विश्रयमाणः । अमतिं । उरूचीं । मर्तभोजनं । अध । रासते । नः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सावित्रे पशौ पुरोडाशस्य हविषोः स घा न इति द्वे अनुवाक्ये । सूत्रे पूर्वमुदाहृतं ॥ आश्वमेधिकेषु सावित्रेष्विषु तृतीयस्यामिष्टाविमे एव याज्यानुवाक्ये । सूत्रितं च । स घा नो देव सविता सहावेति द्वे । आ. १०-६ । इति ॥
> सहावा । तेजोऽंतराण्यभिभावुकं तेजो यस्य सः । वसुपतिर्धनानां पालकः स सविता देवो नोऽस्मभ्यं वसूनि धनान्या साविषत् । आ समंतात्प्रेरयति । घेति पूरणः । स सविता देव उरूचीं विस्तीर्णगमनाममतिं रूपं । दीप्तिमित्यर्थः । विश्रयमाणो निषेवमाणः सन्नधुना नोऽस्मभ्यं मर्तभोजनं मनुष्याणां भोगयोग्यं धनं रासते । ददातु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In the Savitṛ animal sacrifice (the Sāvitra paśu), in the offering of the cake (puroḍāśa) and the oblation, the two verses beginning स घा न (*sa ghā na*) are the two anuvākyā verses; the sūtra was quoted earlier. In the Sāvitra offerings (iṣṭi) of the Aśvamedha, in the third iṣṭi, these same two verses are the yājyā and anuvākyā. It is also laid down in a sūtra: स घा नो देव सविता सहावेति द्वे (*sa ghā no deva savitā sahāveti dve*) (Ā. 10-6).

सहावा (*sahāvā*): he whose energy overpowers other energies. The lord of treasures (वसुपतिः (*vasupatiḥ*)), that god Savitṛ, bestows (आ साविषत् (*ā sāviṣat*), "impels towards") treasures on us; घ (*gha*) is a filler particle. That same Savitṛ, resorting to (विश्रयमाणः (*viśrayamāṇaḥ*), "attending upon") the form (अमतिं (*amatiṃ*)), that is, the radiance, that is wide-reaching (उरूचीं (*urūcīṃ*)), now gives us wealth fit for the enjoyment of men (मर्तभोजनं (*martabhojanaṃ*)); may he give it.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सहावा (*sahāvā*)—he whose radiance pervades everything
- वसुपतिः (*vasupatiḥ*)—the guardian of wealth
- सः (*saḥ*)—the well-known
- सविता देवः (*savitā devaḥ*)—the god Savitṛ
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- वसूनि (*vasūni*)—treasures
- आ साविषत् (*ā sāviṣat*)—may he provide
- उरूचीं (*urūcīṃ*)—widely spread
- अमतिं (*amatiṃ*)—the form
- विश्रयमाणः (*viśrayamāṇaḥ*)—resorting to
- अध (*adha*)—now
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- मर्तभोजनं (*martabhojanaṃ*)—wealth fit for the enjoyment of mortals
- रासते (*rāsate*)—may he give

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May the well-known god Savitṛ, whose radiance pervades everything and who is the guardian of wealth, provide us with treasures. Resorting to the widely spread form, may he now give us wealth fit for the enjoyment of mortals.

##### English translation
May the divine Savitri, who is endowed with energy, the lord of treasure, bestow treasures, upon us concentrating infinite lustre : may he bestow upon us wealth, the source of the enjoyment of mortals.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

The sūtra which says that in the Savitṛ animal sacrifice, in the offering of the cake and of the oblation, this Ṛk beginning स घा नः (*sa ghā naḥ*) and the Ṛk after it (the 3rd Ṛk) are to be recited as anuvākyā mantras has been quoted earlier.

### Printed p. 266
It is explained by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra, स घा नो देवः सविता सहावेति द्वे (*sa ghā no devaḥ savitā sahāveti dve*) (Ā. 10-6), that in the third iṣṭi of the Sāvitra iṣṭis performed in the Aśvamedha sacrifice these two Ṛks are to be recited as yājyā and anuvākyā mantras.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इ॒मा गिरः॑ सवि॒तारं॑ सुजि॒ह्वं पू॒र्णग॑भस्तिमीळते सुपा॒णिं ।  
> चि॒त्रं वयो॑ बृ॒हद॒स्मे द॑धातु यू॒यं पा॑त स्व॒स्तिभिः॒ सदा॑  
> नः ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इमाः । गिरः । सवितारं । सुजिह्वं । पूर्णगभस्तिं । ईळते । सुपाणिं ।  
> चित्रं । वयः । बृहत् । अस्मे इति । दधातु । यूयं । पात । स्वस्तिभिः । सदा । नः ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इमा ईदृग्भूता गिरः । गृणंति स्तुवंतीति गिरः स्तोत्र्यः प्रजाः । यद्वा ।
> इमाः स्तुतिरूपा वाचः । सुजिह्वं शोभनजिह्वं । शोभनवाचमित्यर्थः । पूर्णगभस्तिं संपूर्णधनहस्तं सवितारं देवमीळते । स्तुवंति । स च सविता चित्रं चायनीयं बृहन्महद्वयोऽन्नमस्मे अस्मासु दधातु । यद्वा । अस्मे अस्मभ्यं प्रयच्छतु ।
> हे सवितृप्रमुखा देवाः यूयं नोऽस्मान् स्वस्तिभिः कल्याणैः सदा सर्वदा पात ।
> पालयत ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

इमाः गिरः (*imāḥ giraḥ*): these praises of such a kind. Those who praise are गिरः (*giraḥ*), that is, the praising people; or else इमाः (*imāḥ*) means these words in the form of praise. They praise the god Savitṛ, who is सुजिह्वं (*sujihvaṃ*) ("fair-tongued", that is, of fair speech) and पूर्णगभस्तिं (*pūrṇagabhastiṃ*) (whose hand is full of wealth). May that Savitṛ place in us (अस्मे (*asme*)) wonderful (चित्रं (*citraṃ*), "to be sought after"), abundant food (वयः (*vayaḥ*)); or else अस्मे (*asme*) means "to us", may he give. O gods, with Savitṛ foremost, you protect us always with blessings (स्वस्तिभिः (*svastibhiḥ*), "with auspicious things").

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इमाः गिरः (*imāḥ giraḥ*)—these praises of ours
- पूर्णगभस्तिं (*pūrṇagabhastiṃ*)—him whose hand is filled with complete wealth
- सुजिह्वं (*sujihvaṃ*)—him who has excellent speech
- सुपाणिं (*supāṇiṃ*)—him who has an excellent hand
- सवितारं (*savitāraṃ*)—Savitṛ, the impeller of all
- ईळते (*īḷate*)—praise
- चित्रं (*citraṃ*)—attractive
- बृहत् (*bṛhat*)—great
- वयः (*vayaḥ*)—food
- अस्मे (*asme*)—to us
- दधातु (*dadhātu*)—may (this Savitṛ) provide
- यूयं (*yūyaṃ*)—you (O gods)
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- सदा (*sadā*)—always
- स्वस्तिभिः (*svastibhiḥ*)—with blessings
- पात (*pāta*)—protect

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

These praises of ours praise Savitṛ, whose hand is filled with complete wealth, who has excellent speech, and who is the impeller of all. May this Savitṛ provide us with attractive and great food. O gods, protect us always with blessings.

### Printed p. 267

##### English translation
These praises glorify the eloquent-tongued, dextrous-handed, whose hands are full (of wealth) : may he bestow upon us manifold and abundant food : and do you, (gods), ever cherish us with blessings.

## Sūkta 46 (Maṇḍala 7)
**Introduction**
This sūkta has Rudra as its deity. In the Ṛgveda the place of Rudra is not very prominent**NOTE**; only in the Yajurveda does Rudra have a prominent place. In the Ṛgveda three sūktas, part of one sūkta, and one sūkta in which he is with the deity Soma, only so much praises this deity. The name of Rudra is recited only seventy-five times.

The description of his limbs is as follows. He has a hand (2-33-2), arms (2-33-5; Vā. Saṃ. 16-1) and firm limbs (2-33-11). His lips are beautiful. His body is brown (2-33-5). His form is so radiant that it dazzles the eyes (1-114-5), and he has many forms (2-33-9). He shines like the blazing sun and like gold (1-43-5). He wears golden ornaments (2-33-9) and a necklace of many colours that decays**NOTE** (2-33-10); he sits on the seat of a chariot (2-33-4). In the other Saṃhitās, such as the Vājasaneya, many more characteristics are stated. He is thousand-eyed (A. Ve. 11-2-2 and Vā. Saṃ. 16-7)**NOTE**. He has a belly, a mouth, a tongue and teeth (A. Ve. 11-2-6). His belly is black and his back red (A. Ve. 11-2, 7). His neck is blue (Vā. Saṃ. 16-7) and his hair also blue (A. Ve. 2-27-6). His colour is copper, red (Vā. Saṃ. 16-7). He wears a skin (Vā. Saṃ. 3-61; 16-51) and dwells on a mountain (Vā. Saṃ. 16-2, 3, 4).

He has many weapons capable of attack. He holds the thunderbolt (2-33-3). His arrow passes from the sky especially over the earth (7-46-3). He commonly bears bow and arrows (2-33-10, 11; 5-42-11; 10-125-6). Those arrows are strong and swift (7-46-1). He is slender and is praised together with other bowmen (10-64-8). In the Atharvaveda also he is designated simply as a bowman (A. Ve. 1-28-1; 6-93-1; 15-5-1 to 7). In the Atharvaveda and other parts of the Veda his bow, arrow, weapon, thunderbolt and club are mentioned again and again (A. Ve. 1-28-5; Śa. Brā. 9-1-1-6, etc.).

### Printed p. 268
A point that recurs concerning Rudra is his relation with the Maruts. Rudra is the father of the Maruts (1-114-6, 9; 2-33-1), or the Maruts are said to be sons of Rudra, and they are also called Rudras and Rudriyas. Rudra created the Maruts from the shining udder of the cow Pṛśni (2-34-2). But Rudra and the Maruts nowhere join together as Indra and the Maruts do, who take part in battles and the like in friendship; for Rudra is not always engaged in sacrifice or in slaying demons. The name Tryambaka, often connected with Rudra in the Purāṇas and elsewhere, is also found in the Vedas (Vā. Saṃ. 3-58; Śa. Brā. 2-6-2-9). In the Ṛgveda too, in one place only (7-59-12), he has this name. This word means "having three mothers"; the three mothers here may be the three worlds or the three parts of the universe. In the Purāṇas and elsewhere Ambikā is the wife of Rudra; but in the Vedas she is his sister (Vā. Saṃ. 3-5). The use of the names Umā and Pārvatī, names of Rudra's wife, occurs in the Taittirīya Āraṇyaka and the Kena Upaniṣad.

Rudra is one among the many deities said to be equal to Agni (2-1-6). In other places too he and Agni are one (A. Ve. 8-87-1; Tai. Saṃ. 5-4-3-1; 5-5-7-4; Śa. Brā. 6-1-3-10; 9-1-1-1). The word रुद्र (*rudra*) is often used as an epithet; many times this word is used as an epithet of Agni and of the Aśvin deities. Rudra has two names, Śarva and Bhava (Vā. Saṃ. 16-18). These two names and his destructive arrows are mentioned also in the Atharvaveda (A. Ve. 2-27-6; 6-93-1; 10-1-23; 11-2-1, 12). But here there seems to be the idea that Śarva, Bhava and Rudra are different deities. Śarva and Bhava are sons of Rudra and very fond of hunting (Sāṃ. Śrau. Sū. 4-20-1)**NOTE**. Agni, Aśani, Paśupati, Bhava, Śarva, Īśāna, Mahādeva and Ugradeva are all various forms of one deity (Vā. Saṃ. 39-8). Rudra, Śarva, Paśupati, Ugra, Aśani, Bhava, Īśāna and Mahādeva are the eight forms of Agni (Śa. Brā. 6-1-3-7; Sāṃkhyāyana Brāhmaṇa 6-1)**NOTE**; Śarva, Bhava, Paśupati and Rudra are all names of Agni (Śa. Brā. 1-7-3-8). Of the names above, Aśani is the name of lightning or of the son of Agni (Śa. Brā. 6-1-3-10), but in the Sāṃkhyāyana Brāhmaṇa it is said to be a name of Indra. Cows driven out of the house are most exposed to Rudra's blows; therefore they came under his protection. For this reason Rudra got the name Paśupati.

Rudra is of terrible form (2-33-9, 11; 10-126-5) and destructive like a fierce beast (2-33-11). He is the red-tinged boar of the sky (1-114-5), a bull (2-33-7, 8, 15), of noble nature (7-10-4), mighty (1-43-13; 1-114-1), the strongest of the strong (2-33-3), unassailed (7-46-1); none surpasses him in prowess (2-33-10); he is swift (10-92-5), quick-witted (1-114-4), young (2-33-5), and he does not grow old (6-49-10),

### Printed p. 269
a lord-asura**NOTE** (5-42-11), the great asura of the heavenly world (2-1-6), self-renowned (1-129-3; 10-92-9); he rules over heroes (1-114-1, 2). He is the lord of this world (2-33-9) and its father (6-49-10); he lays down the laws (7-46-1) and by his decrees and his sovereignty over the world he knows all the deeds of gods and men (7-46-2); by his command the rivers flow and moisten the whole earth (10-92-5); he is intelligent (1-43-1), wise (1-114-4) and generous (2-33-7; 6-49-10); therefore he is often called by the name Mīḷhva (generous) (1-114-3), and in the other Vedas the use of the word Mīḷhva applies only to Rudra. He is easily praised (2-33-6) and auspicious (Śiva, 10-92-9).

Doing harm seems to be his special nature. In all the sūktas that praise him, qualities such as fear of his arrow and the evil consequences of his anger are spoken of. In praising him he is entreated not to kill in anger, nor to injure, parents, children, followers, cattle, horses and the like (1-114-7, 8). The worshippers pray that he should turn aside**NOTE** their horses (2-33-1); that he should turn his arrows elsewhere and make others the object of them (2-33-11, 14); that when angry he should turn his weapon away**NOTE** and not trouble those who praise him (6-28-7; 6-46-1, 14). They pray that his weapon, which slays cows, children and men, be kept far from them (2-33-1). The worshippers have entreated that he should not show anger (2-33-4, 5, 6, 15) and should be gracious in the matter of food (10-169-1). There is the epithet "slayer of men" (nṛghna) (4-3-6); the idea is that those men are the enemies of the praisers. He desires to slay men (Ā. Gṛ. 4-8-32)**NOTE**. The matter of his anger is stated still more fully in the other Vedas. Again and again the praisers have entreated him in this matter (Vā. Saṃ. 3-61; A. Ve. 1-28-5, etc.). They beg that the heavenly fire (lightning) should not be hurled at them and that the thunderbolt should be made to fall in another place (A. Ve. 11-2-26; 10-1-23); he also injures men through fever, cough and poison (A. Ve. 11-2-22, 26). He has near him ever-barking dogs with wide mouths, which swallow without chewing the beasts killed in the hunt (A. Ve. 11-2-30; Vā. Saṃ. 16-28). The gods too are greatly afraid, on seeing his strung bow and arrows, that he may destroy them (Śa. Brā. 9-1-1-1, 6). Under the name Mahādeva he slays cows (Tāṇḍya Mahā Brāhmaṇa 6-9-7)**NOTE**. He was created from all terrible things (Ai. Brā. 3-33-1). It is said in the Brāhmaṇas and Sūtra works that it is by his terrible qualities that he stays apart from the other gods. When all the gods went to heaven Rudra stayed behind (Tai. Brā. 1-7-3-1). In Vedic rites, generally after oblations have been offered to the other deities, the remainder is offered to Rudra

### Printed p. 270
(Āśva. Dharma Sū. 2-4-23)**NOTE**. The dwelling of the other deities is in the east, but Rudra dwells in the north (Tai. Saṃ. 6-1-1-1). Because of his fierce nature he does not join particularly with the other deities, and as a paired deity he is praised with Soma in only one sūkta (6-74).

Apart from these, many terrible and harmful qualities of Rudra stated in the Purāṇas and other works are supported in Vedic works such as the Vājasaneya Saṃhitā. But Rudra is not wholly a doer of harm, like the piśācas and others. He is entreated to remove the trouble that may come from other deities (1-114-1, 2; 2-33-6), and he brings welfare to men and animals (1-43-6). It is repeatedly stated that he has the power of curing diseases. He bestows medicines on men (6-46-3). In his hand are excellent medicines (1-114-5); all medicines are under his control (5-42-11), and he has thousands of medicines (7-46-3). His hand itself has the power of curing diseases (2-33-7); by his treatment he makes heroes grow. He is the best of physicians (2-33-4), and with the help of his medicines the worshipper becomes fit to live a hundred years (2-33-2). He is entreated to be auspicious to the offspring of those who worship (7-46-2), that all the villagers be healthy and free from disease, and that he be favourable to cattle and to men (1-114-1). In this connection he has two epithets, Jalāṣa and Jalāṣabheṣaja (1-43-4; A. Ve. 2-27-6). The medicines here may be rain-water. Rudra's lightning and his healing powers are spoken of together (7-46-3). This Rudra of physician's nature is entreated to be gracious along with the other Rudras (7-46-1). Together with Rudra, healing powers are stated also for his sons the Maruts (2-33-13). This description of Rudra's power is stated in the other Vedas too (Vā. Saṃ. 3-59; A. Ve. 2-27-6). But his healing power is not mentioned as often as his destructive power. Many sacrifices to be performed addressed to Rudra for the cure of disease and its removal are stated (Ā. Gṛ. 4-8-40; Kau. Sū. 51-7).

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इमा रुद्रायेति चतुर्ऋचं त्रयोदशं सूक्तं वसिष्ठस्यार्षं रुद्रदेवताकं । अंत्या त्रिष्टुप् शिष्टा जगत्यः । तथा चानुक्रमणिका । इमा रौद्रं त्रिष्टुबंतमिति ॥ शूलगवादिषु रौद्रयज्ञेष्टकेन सूक्तेनोदीची दिगुपस्थेया । सूत्र्यते हि ।
> इमा रुद्राय स्थिरधन्वन इति सर्वरुद्रयज्ञेषु दिशामुपस्थानं । आ. गृ. ४-९-२१
> इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta इमा रुद्राय (*imā rudrāya*), of four Ṛks, is the thirteenth sūkta. It is the vision of Vasiṣṭha, with Rudra as its deity; the last verse is in triṣṭubh and the rest in jagatī. The Anukramaṇikā says accordingly: इमा रौद्रं त्रिष्टुबंतमिति (*imā raudraṃ triṣṭubaṃtamiti*). In rites such as the Śūlagava, with the Raudra yajñeṣṭaka, the northern direction is to be worshipped with this sūkta. For it is laid down in the sūtra: इमा रुद्राय स्थिरधन्वन इति सर्वरुद्रयज्ञेषु दिशामुपस्थानं (*imā rudrāya sthiradhanvana iti sarvarudrayajñeṣu diśāmupasthānaṃ*) (Ā. Gṛ. 4-9-21)**NOTE**.

Anuvāda: This sūkta इमा रुद्राय (*imā rudrāya*) is the thirteenth sūkta in the third anuvāka. It has four Ṛks. Vasiṣṭha is the seer of this sūkta; Rudra is the deity;

### Printed p. 271
The last Ṛk of this sūkta is in the Triṣṭup metre; the remaining Ṛks are in the Jagatī metre. The Anukramaṇikā says इमा रौद्रं त्रिष्टुबंतं (*imā raudraṃ triṣṭubaṃtaṃ*). In Rudra-sacrifices such as the Śūlagava, the northern direction is to be addressed with this sūkta by way of upasthāna. This is set out in the Āśvalāyana Gṛhyasūtra by the sūtra इमा रुद्राय स्थिरधन्वन इति सर्वरुद्रयज्ञेषु दिशामुपस्थानं (*imā rudrāya sthiradhanvana iti sarvarudrayajñeṣu diśāmupasthānaṃ*) (Ā. Gṛ. 4-9-21).

Maṇḍala 7; Anuvāka 3; Sūkta 46. Aṣṭaka 5; Adhyāya 4; Varga 13. Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 4. Seer: Vasiṣṭha. Deity: Rudra. Metre: 1, 2, 3 Jagatī; 4 Triṣṭup.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इ॒मा रु॒द्राय॑ स्थि॒रध॑न्वने॒ गिरः॑ क्षि॒प्रेष॑वे दे॒वाय॑ स्व॒धाव्ने॑ ।  
> अषा॑ळ्हाय॒ सह॑मानाय वे॒धसे॑ ति॒ग्मायु॑धाय भरता शृ॒णोतु॑ नः ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इमाः । रुद्राय । स्थिरधन्वने । गिरः । क्षिप्रइषवे । देवाय । स्वधाव्ने ।  
> अषाळ्हाय । सहमानाय । वेधसे । तिग्मआयुधाय । भरत । शृणोतु । नः ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अस्मदीयाः स्तोतारः यूयमिमा गिरः स्तुती रुद्रायेतन्नामकाय
> देवाय भरत । धारयत । कीदृशाय । स्थिरधन्वने दृढधनुष्काय क्षिप्रेषवे शीघ्रगामिबाणाय स्वधाव्नेन्नवतेषाळ्हाय केनाप्यनभिभूताय सहमानाय शत्रूणामभिभवित्रे वेधसे विधात्रे तिग्मायुधाय तीक्ष्णास्त्राय । एवंभूताय रुद्राय
> स्तुतीर्भरत । स च रुद्रो नोऽस्मदीयाः स्तुतीः शृणोतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O our praisers, bear these praises to the god named Rudra. He is of what kind? He has a firm bow (स्थिरधन्वने (*sthiradhanvane*)), fast-flying arrows (क्षिप्रेषवे (*kṣipreṣave*)), is स्वधाव्ने (*svadhāvne*) "possessed of food", अषाळ्हाय (*aṣāḷhāya*) "unovercome by anyone", सहमानाय (*sahamānāya*) "the overcomer of enemies", वेधसे (*vedhase*) "the creator", तिग्मायुधाय (*tigmāyudhāya*) "of sharp weapons". Bring praises to Rudra so characterised, and may that Rudra hear our praises.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इमाः गिरः (*imāḥ giraḥ*)—these praises
- स्थिरधन्वने (*sthiradhanvane*)—to him who has a firm bow
- क्षिप्रेषवे (*kṣipreṣave*)—who has swift-going arrows
- स्वधाव्ने (*svadhāvne*)—who has food
- अषाळ्हाय (*aṣāḷhāya*)—the unresisted
- सहमानाय (*sahamānāya*)—who has the power to defeat enemies
- वेधसे (*vedhase*)—the creator of the world

### Printed p. 272
- तिग्मायुधाय (*tigmāyudhāya*)—who has sharp weapons
- देवाय (*devāya*)—the divine one
- रुद्राय (*rudrāya*)—to Rudra, to offer
- भरत (*bharata*)—bear
- नः (*naḥ*)—our praises
- शृणोतु (*śṛṇotu*)—may he hear

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O praisers, fashion and offer these praises to Rudra, who has a firm bow, swift-going arrows, food, who is unresisted, has the power to defeat enemies, is the maker of the world, has sharp weapons, and is divine in nature. May that same Rudra hear our praises.

##### English translation
Offer these praises to the divine Rudra, armed with the strong bow and fast-flying arrows, the bestower of food, the invincible, the conqueror, the creator, the wielder of sharp weapons : may he hear our (praises).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Yāska explains the derivation of the word Rudra thus:
रुद्रो रौतीति सतः । रोरूयमाणो द्रवतीति वा रोदयतेर्वा । यदरुदत्तद्रुद्रस्य रुद्रत्वं इति काठकं । यदरोदीत्तद्रुद्रस्य रुद्रत्वं इति हारिद्रविकं । तस्यैषा भवति ॥ (*rudro rautīti sataḥ | rorūyamāṇo dravatīti vā rodayatervā | yadarudattadrudrasya rudratvaṃ iti kāṭhakaṃ | yadarodīttadrudrasya rudratvaṃ iti hāridravikaṃ | tasyaiṣā bhavati ‖*) (Ni. 10-6)
The word Rudra may be formed from the root रौति (*rauti*), meaning to cry or roar loudly; or from the root द्रवति (*dravati*) preceded by रु (*ru*), meaning to run crying; or from the root रुद् (*rud*), meaning to weep. On this point the Kāṭhaka śruti यदरुदत्तद्रुद्रस्य रुद्रत्वं (*yadarudattadrudrasya rudratvaṃ*) is an example: because this god roared loudly, he got the name Rudra. Also the Hāridravika śruti sentence यदरोदीत्तद्रुद्रस्य रुद्रत्वं (*yadarodīttadrudrasya rudratvaṃ*) may be cited as authority: because he roared, this god got the name Rudra; roaring is his natural quality. In the Ṛgveda, a Ṛk that tells of this god Rudra is given here as an example:
इमा रुद्राय स्थिरधन्वने गिरः क्षिप्रेषवे देवाय स्वधाव्ने । अषाळ्हाय सहमानाय वेधसे तिग्मायुधाय भरता शृणोतु नः ॥ (*imā rudrāya sthiradhanvane giraḥ kṣipreṣave devāya svadhāvne | aṣāḷhāya sahamānāya vedhase tigmāyudhāya bharatā śṛṇotu naḥ ‖*) (Mai. Saṃ. 7-86-1)
इमा रुद्राय दृढधन्वने गिरः क्षिप्रेषवे देवायान्नवतेऽषाढायान्नैः । सहमानाय विधात्रे तिग्मायुधाय भरत शृणोतु नः । (*imā rudrāya dṛḍhadhanvane giraḥ kṣipreṣave devāyānnavate 'ṣāḍhāyānnaiḥ | sahamānāya vidhātre tigmāyudhāya bharata śṛṇotu naḥ |*)

### Printed p. 273
तिग्मं तेजतेरुत्साहकर्मणः । आयुधमायोधनात् । तस्यैषा परा भवति ॥ (*tigmaṃ tejaterutsāhakarmaṇaḥ | āyudhamāyodhanāt | tasyaiṣā parā bhavati ‖*) (Ni. 10-6)
Offer these words of praise to the god Rudra. His bow is firm. The arrows leaving his bow travel very swiftly. He is rich in food: that is, he has abundant food and drink to give to those who praise him. He harms his enemies, but he himself is unassailed. He is the regulator of the actions that people perform. His weapon is very sharp. May such a god listen to our praises: such is the purport of the Nirukta.
The word तिग्म (*tigma*) is formed from the root तीज् (*tīj*) "to sharpen" and means very sharp. आयुध (*āyudha*) means the instrument by which one fights and defeats enemies. Another example concerning this Rudra will be given (see the Special Topics of the third Ṛk of this sūkta).

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स हि क्षये॑ण॒ क्षम्य॑स्य॒ जन्म॑नः॒ साम्रा॑ज्येन दि॒व्यस्य॒ चेत॑ति ।  
> अव॒न्नवं॑ती॒रुप॑ नो॒ दुर॑श्चरानमी॒वो रु॑द्र॒ जासु॑ नो भव ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । हि । क्षयेण । क्षम्यस्य । जन्मनः । साम्राज्येन । दिव्यस्य । चेतति ।  
> अवन् । अवंतीः । उप । नः । दुरः । चर । अनमीवः । रुद्र । जासु । नः । भव ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> स हि खलु रुद्रो देवः क्षम्यस्य क्षमायां पृथिव्यां भवः । तस्य जन्मनो
> जनस्य क्षयेणैश्वर्येण चेतति प्रज्ञायते । दिव्यस्य जनस्य च साम्राज्येनैश्वर्येण
> प्रज्ञायते । शेषः प्रत्यक्षकृतः । हे रुद्र देव त्वं चावंतीस्त्वां स्तोत्रैः स्पर्पयंतीर्नोऽस्मदीयाः प्रजा अवन् पालयन् दुरो मर्यादा अस्मदीयानि गृहाण्युप चर । उपगच्छ । किंच त्वं नोऽस्मदीयासु प्रजास्वनमीवः । अमीवा रोगः ।
> तामकुर्वन् भव ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

That Rudra is क्षम्यस्य (*kṣamyasya*), "born on earth (क्षमा (*kṣamā*))"; he is known by his lordship (क्षयेण (*kṣayeṇa*)) over the people of earth, and by his sovereignty (साम्राज्येन (*sāmrājyena*)) over the people of heaven (दिव्यस्य (*divyasya*)). The rest is to be supplied. O Rudra, protecting (अवन् (*avan*)) our people who propitiate you with hymns, visit our homes (दुरः (*duraḥ*)). And be अनमीवः (*anamīvaḥ*), making no अमीवा (*amīvā*) (disease) among our offspring.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- स हि (*sa hi*)—that same Rudra
- क्षम्यस्य (*kṣamyasya*)—of those born on earth
- जन्मनः (*janmanaḥ*)—for the people
- क्षयेण (*kṣayeṇa*)—through his wealth
- चेतति (*cetati*)—becomes perceptible
- दिव्यस्य (*divyasya*)—for the people of the world of the gods
- साम्राज्येन (*sāmrājyena*)—

### Printed p. 274
- through his lordship is he known
- रुद्र (*rudra*)—O Rudra
- अवंतीः (*avaṃtīḥ*)—who satisfy you with praises
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- अवन् (*avan*)—protecting
- दुरः (*duraḥ*)—to our homes
- उप चर (*upa cara*)—come and be present
- नः जासु (*naḥ jāsu*)—among the people belonging to us
- अनमीवः (*anamīvaḥ*)—as the preventer of disease
- भव (*bhava*)—be

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Rudra is known to those born on earth in the form of his wealth, and to those born in the heavenly world through his lordship. O Rudra, protect us who satisfy you with praises, and come to our homes. Be a preventer of disease among the people who belong to us.

##### English translation
He is known by his rule over those of terrestrial birth, by his sovereignty over those of celestial (origin) : protecting our progeny, Rudra, propitiating you (by praise), come to our dwellings, and be to them a guardian against disease.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> या ते॑ दि॒द्युदव॑सृष्टा दि॒वस्परि॑ क्ष्म॒या चर॑ति॒ परि॒ सा वृ॑णक्तु नः ।  
> स॒हस्रं॑ ते स्वपिवात भेष॒जा मा न॑स्तो॒केषु॒ तन॑येषु रीरिषः ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> या । ते । दिद्युत् । अवसृष्टा । दिवः । परि । क्ष्मया । चरति । परि । सा । वृणक्तु । नः ।  
> सहस्रं । ते । सु‌अपिवात । भेषजा । मा । नः । तोकेषु । तनयेषु । रीरिषः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे रुद्र ते दैव्यैतात्मनस्तव संबंधिनी दिवस्पर्यंतरिक्षप्रकाशादवसृष्टा निमुक्ता या दिद्युदशनिरूपा हेतिः क्ष्मया क्षित्या क्षितौ वा चरति वर्तते सा दिद्युन्नोऽस्मान् परि वृणक्तु । परित्यजतु । अपि च हे स्वपिवात ते तव सहस्रं बहूनि भेषजा
> भेषजानि यान्यौषधानि संति तान्यस्मभ्यं प्रयच्छेति शेषः । नोऽस्माकं तोकेषु
> पुत्रेषु तनयेषु च मा रीरिषः । हिंसां मा कृथाः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Rudra, the lightning-weapon (दिद्युत् (*didyut*)) of you, of divine nature, discharged (अवसृष्टा (*avasṛṣṭā*)) from heaven's radiance in the atmosphere, which moves over the earth (क्ष्मया (*kṣmayā*) meaning क्षित्या (*kṣityā*) or क्षितौ (*kṣitau*)): may it pass us by (परि वृणक्तु (*pari vṛṇaktu*) = परित्यजतु (*parityajatu*)). And, O स्वपिवात (*svapivāta*), your thousand medicaments: give them to us (this is to be supplied). Do not harm (मा रीरिषः (*mā rīriṣaḥ*)) our sons (तोकेषु (*tokeṣu*)) and grandsons (तनयेषु (*tanayeṣu*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Rudra) ते (*te*)—that which belongs to you
- दिवस्परि (*divaspari*)—from the atmosphere
- अवसृष्टा (*avasṛṣṭā*)—and is discharged
- या विद्युत् (*yā vidyut*)—which lightning-form weapon
- क्ष्मया (*kṣmayā*)—over the earth
- चरति (*carati*)—

### Printed p. 275
- moves about
- सा (*sā*)—that same weapon of power
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- परि वृणक्तु (*pari vṛṇaktu*)—let it leave alone
- स्वपिवाते (*svapivāte*)—O Rudra, of beneficent conduct
- ते (*te*)—with you
- सहस्रं (*sahasraṃ*)—in great abundance
- भेषजा (*bheṣajā*)—medicines are
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- तोकेषु (*tokeṣu*)—among sons
- तनयेषु (*tanayeṣu*)—among their sons (grandsons)
- मा रीरिषः (*mā rīriṣaḥ*)—do not cause harm

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Rudra, may that lightning-form weapon of yours, discharged from the atmosphere, which moves over the earth, leave us alone. O Rudra of beneficent conduct, you have many kinds of medicines. Do not cause harm to our sons and their sons.

##### English translation
May your blazing (weapon). which, discharged from heaven traverses the earth, avoid us : yours, appeaser of the wind, are a thousand medicaments: inflict not evil upon our sons and grandsons.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In explaining the derivation and meaning of the word Rudra, Yāska takes this Ṛk as an example and gives the Nirukta for it also:
या ते दिद्युदवसृष्टा दिवस्परि क्ष्मया चरति परि सा वृणक्तु नः । सहस्रं ते स्वपिवात भेषजा मा नस्तोकेषु तनयेषु रीरिषः ॥ (*yā te didyudavasṛṣṭā divaspari kṣmayā carati pari sā vṛṇaktu naḥ | sahasraṃ te svapivāta bheṣajā mā nastokeṣu tanayeṣu rīriṣaḥ ‖*)
तोकं तुद्यतेः । तनयं तनोतेः ॥ (*tokaṃ tudyateḥ | tanayaṃ tanoteḥ ‖*)
अग्निरपि रुद्र उच्यते । तस्यैषा भवति । (*agnirapi rudra ucyate | tasyaiṣā bhavati |*)
जराबोध तद्विविड्ढि विशेविशे यज्ञियाय । स्तोमं रुद्राय दृशीकं ॥ जरा स्तुति........दर्शनीयं ॥ (*jarābodha tadviviḍḍhi viśeviśe yajñiyāya | stomaṃ rudrāya dṛśīkaṃ ‖ jarā stuti........darśanīyaṃ ‖*) (Ni. 10-1)
O Rudra, may your shining weapon, hurled from above the heavenly world, not touch us. The word दिद्युत् (*didyut*) may be formed from the root द्यति (*dyati*) meaning to cut, or from the root द्युति (*dyuti*) meaning to apply or to harm, or from the root द्युत् (*dyut*) (द्योतति (*dyotati*)) meaning to shine. The word means "shining". क्ष्मया चरति (*kṣmayā carati*): क्षा (*kṣā*) is the earth; क्ष्मया चरति (*kṣmayā carati*) means it moves over the earth or along with the earth. Or it means that it moves causing destruction. May such a destructive weapon of Rudra pass over us without harming us or touching us. (स्वपिवात (*svapivāta*) — स्वाप्तवचन (*svāptavacana*))

### Printed p. 276
O Rudra of beneficial words, you have thousands of medicines. Do not harm our sons and grandsons. The word तोकं (*tokaṃ*) is formed from the root तुद् (*tud*) (to push, thrust, impel), meaning "do this", "impel", and signifies a son: a son who is impelled by his father to do such and such a work. The word तनय (*tanaya*) is formed from the root तनु विस्तारे (*tanu vistāre*), meaning to extend, and signifies a grandson: one who extends and increases the family.
Agni too is on some occasions called Rudra. On this point the Ṛk जराबोध तद्विविड्ढि विशेविशे यज्ञियाय । स्तोमं रुद्राय दृशीकं (*jarābodha tadviviḍḍhi viśeviśe yajñiyāya | stomaṃ rudrāya dṛśīkaṃ*) is itself the example: this is the main purport of the Nirukta.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> मा नो॑ वधी रुद्र॒ मा परा॑ दा॒ मा ते॑ भूम॒ प्रसि॑तौ हीळि॒तस्य॑ ।  
> आ नो॑ भज ब॒र्हिषि॑ जीवशं॒से यू॒यं पा॑त स्व॒स्तिभिः॒ सदा॑ नः ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> मा । नः । वधीः । रुद्र । मा । परा । दाः । मा । ते । भूम । प्रसितौ । हीळितस्य ।  
> आ । नः । भज । बर्हिषि । जीवशंसे । यूयं । पात । स्वस्तिभिः । सदा । नः ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे रुद्र त्वं नोऽस्मान् मा वधीः । मा हिंसीः । तथा मा परा दाः । मा च त्याक्षीः । अपि च हीळितस्य क्रुद्धस्य ते तव प्रसितौ प्रकर्षेण बंधने वयं च मा भूम । किंच जीवशंसे जीवैराशंसनीये बर्हिषि यज्ञे नोऽस्माना भज । भागिनः कुरु । हे रुद्रप्रमुखा देवाः यूयं नोऽस्मान्कल्याणैः सर्वदा पालयत ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Rudra, do not slay (मा वधीः (*mā vadhīḥ*)) us; do not give us up (मा परा दाः (*mā parā dāḥ*) = do not abandon). Nor let us be in the bond (प्रसितौ (*prasitau*), in binding fast) of you when angered (हीळितस्य (*hīḷitasya*)). Further, grant us a share (आ भज (*ā bhaja*) = make us sharers) in the sacrifice (बर्हिषि (*barhiṣi*)) which is to be wished for by the living (जीवशंसे (*jīvaśaṃse*)). O gods led by Rudra, protect us always with blessings.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- रुद्र (*rudra*)—O Rudra
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- मा वधीः (*mā vadhīḥ*)—do not harm
- मा परा दाः (*mā parā dāḥ*)—do not give up to enemies
- हीळितस्य (*hīḷitasya*)—of you who are angered
- ते (*te*)—your
- प्रसितौ (*prasitau*)—in the bond
- मा भूम (*mā bhūma*)—let us not be caught
- जीवशंसे (*jīvaśaṃse*)—desired for life
- बर्हिषि (*barhiṣi*)—in the sacrifice
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- आ भज (*ā bhaja*)—make sharers
- (O gods) यूयं (*yūyaṃ*)—you
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- सदा (*sadā*)—always
- स्वस्तिभिः (*svastibhiḥ*)—with blessings
- पात (*pāta*)—protect

### Printed p. 277

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Rudra, do not harm us. Do not abandon us to our enemies. Let us not fall into the bondage of you when you are angered. Make us sharers in the sacrifice, which is desired for life and begun by men. O gods, may you always protect us with blessings.

##### English translation
Harm us not, Rudra : abandon us not : let us not fall under the bond-age of you when displeased : make us partakers of the life-promoting sacrifice : and do you, (gods), ever cherish us with blessings.

## Sūkta 47 (Maṇḍala 7)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> आपो यं व इति चतुर्ऋचं चतुर्दशं सूक्तं वसिष्ठस्यार्षं त्रैष्टुभमब्देवताकं । आपो यमापमित्यनुक्रांतं च ॥ गतो विनियोगः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta आपो यं व (*āpo yaṃ va*) has four Ṛks and is the fourteenth sūkta. Its seer is Vasiṣṭha, its metre is Triṣṭubh and its deity is the Waters. The Anukramaṇī says: आपो यमापं (*āpo yamāpaṃ*). The viniyoga has already been stated.

##### Anuvāda

The sūkta beginning आपो यं वः (*āpo yaṃ vaḥ*) is the fourteenth sūkta in the third anuvāka. It has four Ṛks. Vasiṣṭha is the seer of this sūkta; Āpaḥ (the Waters) is the deity; Triṣṭubh is the metre. In the Anukramaṇī it is said आपो यमापं (*āpo yamāpaṃ*). The viniyoga has been stated earlier.

##### Particulars

Sūkta 47. Maṇḍala 7; Anuvāka 3; Sūkta 47. Aṣṭaka 5; Adhyāya 4; Varga 14. Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 4. Seer: Vasiṣṭha. Deity: Āpaḥ. Metre: Triṣṭubh.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आपो॒ यं वः॑ प्रथ॒मं दे॑व॒यंत॑ इंद्र॒पान॑मू॒र्मिर्कृ॑ण्वते॒ळः ।  
> तं वो॑ व॒यं शुचि॑मरि॒प्रम॒द्य घृ॑त॒प्रुषं॒ मधु॑मंतं वनेम

### Printed p. 278

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आपः । यं । वः । प्रथमं । देवयंतः । इंद्रपानं । ऊर्मिं । अकृण्वत । इळः ।  
> तं । वः । वयं । शुचिं । अरिप्रं । अद्य । घृतप्रुषं । मधुमंतं । वनेम ॥१॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> देवयंतो देवानिच्छंतोऽध्वर्यवो हे आपो हे अब्देवताः वो युष्माकं कार्यभूतमिंद्रपानमिंद्रेण पातव्यमिळ इळाया भूम्याः संभूतं यमूर्मिं सोमाख्यं यं रसं प्रथमं पुराकृण्वत अभिषवणवचनादिभिः समस्कुर्वत अद्येदानीं वयमपि वो युष्मदीयं तमूर्मिं वनेम । संभजेमहि । कीदृशं । शुचिं शुद्धमरिप्रं पापरहितं घृतप्रुषं वृष्टिलक्षणमुदकं सिंचंतं मधुमंतं मधुररसोपेतं । एवंभूतं तं वनेमेति संबंधः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The priests (adhvaryus), desiring the gods, are addressed to the Waters. The essence called Soma, to be drunk by Indra and sprung from the earth (इळाया भूम्याः (*iḷāyā bhūmyāḥ*)), is your product. This ūrmi, which they first prepared by pressing and the like, is now to be shared by us as well. Sāyaṇa glosses it as pure (शुचिं (*śuciṃ*)), faultless (अरिप्रं (*aripraṃ*), free from sin), घृतप्रुषं (*ghṛtapruṣaṃ*) (sprinkling water in the form of rain) and मधुमंतं (*madhumaṃtaṃ*) (endowed with sweet essence). The construction is: let us enjoy (वनेम (*vanema*), "may we share in") that very ūrmi.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- आपः (*āpaḥ*)—O gods of the waters
- वः (*vaḥ*)—belonging to you
- इंद्रपानं (*iṃdrapānaṃ*)—fit for Indra's drinking
- इळः (*iḷaḥ*)—sprung from the earth
- यं ऊर्मिं (*yaṃ ūrmiṃ*)—which Soma-essence
- प्रथमं (*prathamaṃ*)—first
- देवयंतः (*devayaṃtaḥ*)—the adhvaryus who desire to worship the gods
- अकृण्वत (*akṛṇvata*)—produced by pressing and the like
- तं (*taṃ*)—that same
- वः (*vaḥ*)—pertaining to you
- शुचिं (*śuciṃ*)—pure
- अरिप्रं (*aripraṃ*)—free from sin
- घृतप्रुषं (*ghṛtapruṣaṃ*)—shedding rain
- मधुमंतं (*madhumaṃtaṃ*)—endowed with a sweet essence, that Soma
- अद्य (*adya*)—at this time of the sacrifice
- वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we also
- वनेम (*vanema*)—may we worship (may we obtain)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O gods of the waters, the essence in the form of Soma that belongs to you, fit for Indra's drinking and sprung from the earth, which the adhvaryus, desirous of worshipping the gods, first produced by pressing and the like—that same Soma, belonging to you, pure, free from sin, shedding rain and endowed with a sweet essence: may we also worship it at this time of the sacrifice (may we drink it).

##### English translation
We solicit from you, waters, to-day, that pure, faultless, rain-shedding, sweet essence of the earth, which the devout have first consecrated as the beverage of Indra.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> तमू॒र्मिमा॑पो॒ मधु॑मत्तमं वो॒ऽपां नपा॑दवत्वाशु॒हेमा॑ ।

### Printed p. 279

> यस्मिन्निंद्रो वसुभिर्मादयाते तमश्याम देवयंतो वो  
> अद्य ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> शं । ऊर्मिं । आपः । मधुमत्‌तमं । वः । आपां । नपात् । अवतु । आशुहेमा  
> यस्मिन् । इंद्रः । वसुभिः । मादयाते । शं । अश्याम । देवयंतः । वः । अद्य ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे आप एतत्संज्ञका देवाः वो युष्मदीयं मधुमत्तमं रसवत्तमं तमूर्मिं प्रसिद्धं सोमाख्यं रसमाशुहेमा शीघ्रगतिरपां नपादिति नपात्संज्ञको दे-वोऽवतु । पालयतु । इंद्रो यस्मिन्नूर्मौ वसुभिर्वासकैर्देवैः सह मादयाते माद्येत् अद्यास्मिन्यागे देवयंतो देवकामा वयं वो युष्मदीयं तमूर्मि-मश्याम । प्राप्नुयाम ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Waters, may the swift-moving god called Apāṃnapāt protect that well-known, sweetest, most flavoursome ūrmi, the essence called Soma, which is yours. In that essence Indra rejoices together with the Vasus, the gods who give dwelling. To-day, at this sacrifice, may we, desirous of the gods, attain that ūrmi of yours.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- आपः (*āpaḥ*)—O gods of the waters
- वः (*vaḥ*)—belonging to you
- तं (*taṃ*)—well-known
- मधुमत्तमं (*madhumattamaṃ*)—most delicious
- ऊर्मिं (*ūrmiṃ*)—the essence in the form of Soma
- आशुहेमा (*āśuhemā*)—the swift-moving
- अपां नपात् (*apāṃ napāt*)—the god called Apāṃnapāt
- अवतु (*avatu*)—may he protect
- यस्मिन् (*yasmin*)—in which Soma-essence
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- वसुभिः (*vasubhiḥ*)—together with the gods called Vasus
- मादयाते (*mādayāte*)—rejoices
- वः (*vaḥ*)—belonging to you
- शं (*śaṃ*)—that same essence
- अद्य (*adya*)—on this day of the sacrifice
- देवयंतः (*devayaṃtaḥ*)—we also, desirous of worshipping the gods
- अश्याम (*aśyāma*)—may we obtain

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O gods of the waters, may the swift-moving god called Apāṃnapāt protect the well-known, most delicious Soma-essence that belongs to you. That same essence of yours, in which Indra rejoices together with the gods called Vasus, may we also, desirous of worshipping the gods, obtain on this day of the sacrifice.

##### English translation
May the swift-moving grandson of the waters protect, waters, your most sweet essence wherewith may Indra and the Vasus be delighted : and may we, devoted to the gods, partake (of it).

### Printed p. 280

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Apāṃnapāt—for this word Yāska says: अपां नपात्तनूनप्त्रा व्याख्यातः (*apāṃ napāttanūnaptrā vyākhyātaḥ*) (Ni. 9-20); नपादित्यनंतरायाः प्रजाया नामधेयं निर्णेततमा भवति (*napādityanaṃtarāyāḥ prajāyā nāmadheyaṃ nirṇetatamā bhavati*) (Ni. 8-6). The derivation of the word Apāṃnapāt was explained in the course of explaining the word Tanūnapāt. Here the two separate words अपां (*apāṃ*) and नपात् (*napāt*) combine into the word Apāṃnapāt. Apāṃ means "of the waters". Napāt is the name for the offspring born after a son, that is, a grandson. The meaning is "grandson of the waters". This word is generally said of Agni. As follows:

आपोऽक्र तन्ह उच्यंते । तता अंतरिक्षे ताभ्य ओषधिवनस्पतयो जायंत ओषधिवनस्पतिभ्यो ऽग्निर्जायते ॥ (*āpo 'kra tanha ucyaṃte | tatā aṃtarikṣe tābhya oṣadhivanaspatayo jāyaṃta oṣadhivanaspatibhyo 'gnirjāyate ‖*) (Ni. 8-6)

From the waters the herbs and trees (plants, trees and so on) arise. From the herbs and trees (tree, wood, kindling-sticks) Agni arises. Here, kindling Agni by rubbing wooden kindling-sticks, those of the tree, is the custom in sacrifices and the like; therefore it is said that Agni is born in the herbs and trees. Thus, since the tree arises from the waters and Agni from the tree, Agni became the grandson of the waters. In the Ṛgveda Apāṃnapāt is regarded as a separate deity, said to be a special form of Agni.

The Ṛgveda has one separate sūkta (2-35) and two separate Ṛks (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-30-3, 4) on this deity. The name of this deity is recited about thirty times.

It is necessary to state some matters about this deity. The waters stood around the radiant deity Apāṃnapāt. The waters surround this youth. Three goddesses wish to give food to him, the excellent one. He drinks the milk of those mothers. (2-35-3, 5) In the form of a bull he placed the embryo in them. In the form of a child he drinks their milk; they kiss him. He grows in the water itself and shines. He blazes in the water without the help of fuel and the like. (10-30-4). He wears lightning as his garment. The swift-flowing, golden-coloured water bears him and whirls round in a circle. (1-95-4, 5) His body shines with golden lustre. Born of a golden womb, he grants food to those who worship him. Dwelling in the loftiest region he shines without fading. The swiftly flowing waters bring ghee for the food of their son and flow around, clothed in garments. His face is of the colour of gold; maidens make it shine. Ghee is his food. He grows in a way that no one notices. In his house there is a cow that yields good milk. Horses swift as thought

### Printed p. 281

bear him. He has a connection with the rivers. It is he who caused all beings to be born; all those beings are his parts. In the last Ṛk of the sūkta addressed to this deity it is said that he is Agni. In many sūktas addressed to Agni also, Agni is called Apāṃnapāt (Vā. Saṃ. 8-24).

The name of the deity Apāṃnapāt is recited together with many deities. He is praised along with the deities Ajaikapāt (2-31-6; 7-35-13), Ahirbudhnya (1-186-5, 2-31-6, 7-35-13) and Savitṛ (2-31-6, 6-50-13). Since Savitṛ too is a special form of Agni, that Savitṛ is also once (1-22-6) called by the word Apāṃnapāt. The deity Apāṃnapāt—golden-coloured, clad in lightning, dwelling in the loftiest region, growing in a hidden manner, shining forth in rawness, the son of the water, descending to the earth, and regarded as equal to Agni—may be the very Agni in the form of lightning hidden in the clouds. Agni is called Apāṃnapāt and also the embryo of the waters (अपां गर्भः (*apāṃ garbhaḥ*)) (7-9-23; 10-27-03); in that very form he is established in the houses of men. (3-5-3) His dwelling is in the water itself (8-43-9). Agni, who is the embryo of plants and of water, is born from the kindling-sticks (3-1-13). Since Agni is called अद्रीः सूनुं (*adrīḥ sūnuṃ*)—son of the mountain (10-20-7)—the lightning born of the mountain-shaped cloud must be the son of the mountain. From descriptions such as that the third form of Agni blazes in the water, in the ocean, in the region of the sky, or in the lap of the water (10-45-1, 2, 3), the deity Apāṃnapāt must be what is meant everywhere here. This deity has given occasion to various opinions: a deity of water, Agni born in water, the sun, lightning, and so on.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> श॒तप॑वित्राः स्व॒धया॒ मदं॑तीर्दे॒वीर्दे॒वाना॒मपि॑ यंति॒ पाथः॑ ।  
> ता इंद्र॑स्य॒ न मि॑नंति व्र॒तानि॒ सिंधु॑भ्यो ह॒व्यं घृ॒तव॑ज्जुहोत ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> शतपवित्राः । स्वधया । मदंतीः । देवीः । देवानां । अपि । यंति । पाथः ।  
> ताः । इंद्रस्य । न । मिनंति । व्रतानि । सिंधुभ्यः । हव्यं । घृतवत् । जुहोत ॥ ४ ॥

**NOTE**

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> शतपवित्राः शतं बहूनि पवित्राणि शोधनानि रूपाणि यासां ताः स्वधया स्वकीयभूतेनान्नेन मदंतीर्जनान्मादयंत्यो देवीर्देव्यो द्योतमाना

### Printed p. 282

> आपो देवानामिंद्रादीनां पाथः स्थानमपि यंति । प्रविशंति । तास्तादृश्य आप इंद्रस्य प्रीणनानि व्रतानि यज्ञादीनि कर्माणि न मिनंति । न हिंसंति । उत्पादयंतीत्यर्थः । हे अध्वर्यवः यूयं सिंधुभ्यस्ताभ्योऽद्भ्यो घृतवदुपस्तरणाभिघारणलक्षणाज्ययुक्तं हव्यं पुरोडाशादिकं हविर्जुहोत । जुहुत ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The Waters have a hundred, that is many, purifying forms (शतपवित्राः (*śatapavitrāḥ*)). They delight people with their own food (स्वधया (*svadhayā*)), and are divine, shining. They enter the abode (पाथः (*pāthaḥ*)) of the gods, Indra and the others. Those same Waters do not impair (न मिनंति (*na minaṃti*), do not harm; that is, they bring about) the rites such as sacrifices, which please Indra. O adhvaryus, offer to those Waters, the rivers (सिंधुभ्यः (*siṃdhubhyaḥ*)), the oblation (हव्यं (*havyaṃ*)), the puroḍāśa and the like, endowed with ghee (घृतवत् (*ghṛtavat*)), that is, with ghee used for the spreading and the pouring over.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- शतपवित्राः (*śatapavitrāḥ*)—having many purifying forms
- स्वधया (*svadhayā*)—with food produced by themselves
- मदंतीः (*madaṃtīḥ*)—which rejoice
- देवीः (*devīḥ*)—shining waters
- देवानां (*devānāṃ*)—of the gods, Indra and the others
- पाथः (*pāthaḥ*)—the abode
- अपि यंति (*api yaṃti*)—they enter
- ताः (*tāḥ*)—those same waters
- इंद्रस्य (*iṃdrasya*)—of Indra
- व्रतानि (*vratāni*)—the rites such as sacrifices that please him
- न मिनंति (*na minaṃti*)—do not harm
- (O adhvaryus) सिंधुभ्यः (*siṃdhubhyaḥ*)—to those same waters in the form of streams
- घृतवत् (*ghṛtavat*)—filled with ghee
- हव्यं (*havyaṃ*)—the oblation
- जुहोत (*juhota*)—offer in the form of an offering

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The waters, having many purifying forms, rejoicing in food produced by themselves, and shining, enter the abode of Indra and the other gods. Those same waters do not harm the rites such as sacrifices that please Indra. O adhvaryus, offer an oblation filled with ghee, in the form of an offering, to those same waters in the form of streams.

##### English translation
The divine Waters, the purifiers of many, gratifying men with food, pursue the paths of the gods: they impede not the sacred rites of Indra: offer, (priests), the butter-charged oblation to the rivers.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

शतपवित्राः (*śatapavitrāḥ*)—शतं बहूनि पवित्राणि पावनानि रूपाणि यासां ताः (*śataṃ bahūni pavitrāṇi pāvanāni rūpāṇi yāsāṃ tāḥ*): waters that have many kinds of purifying forms—this is the commentator's explanation. The word pavitra has many meanings. Yāska explains them thus:

पवित्रं पुनातेः । मंत्रः पवित्रमुच्यते । येन देवाः पवि-त्रेणात्मानं पुनते सदा इत्यपि निगमो भवति ॥ (*pavitraṃ punāteḥ | maṃtraḥ pavitramucyate | yena devāḥ pavi-treṇātmānaṃ punate sadā ityapi nigamo bhavati ‖*) (Ni. 5-6)

The word pavitra is formed from the root punāti, meaning "to purify". Since a mantra purifies those who recite it, pavitra means a mantra. In this matter the Vedic sentence येन देवाः पवित्रेणात्मानं पुनते सदा (*yena devāḥ pavitreṇātmānaṃ punate sadā*) is the example. In this sentence pavitreṇa means "by a mantra". The sense of the sentence is: the mantra, by whose power of recitation the gods make themselves pure.

### Printed p. 283

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

The rays are also called pavitra. On this point the two Vedic passages already quoted above, beginning गभस्तिपूतः (*gabhastipūtaḥ*), serve as examples.

आपः पवित्रमुच्यंते । शतपवित्राः स्वधया मदंतीः । बहूदकाः (*āpaḥ pavitramucyaṃte | śatapavitrāḥ svadhayā madaṃtīḥ | bahūdakāḥ*) (Ni. 5-6)

The word pavitra also means waters. The sentence of the present Ṛk, शतपवित्राः (*śatapavitrāḥ*), is itself the Vedic example on this point. Here शतपवित्राः (*śatapavitrāḥ*) means abundant waters.

अग्निः पवित्रमुच्यते । वायुः पवित्रमुच्यते । सोमः पवित्रमुच्यते । सूर्यः पवित्रमुच्यते । इंद्रः पवित्रमुच्यते । अग्निः पवित्रं स मा पुनातु वायुः सोमः सूर्य इंद्रः पवित्रं ते मा पुनंतु इत्यपि निगमो भवति ॥ (*agniḥ pavitramucyate | vāyuḥ pavitramucyate | somaḥ pavitramucyate | sūryaḥ pavitramucyate | iṃdraḥ pavitramucyate | agniḥ pavitraṃ sa mā punātu vāyuḥ somaḥ sūrya iṃdraḥ pavitraṃ te mā punaṃtu ityapi nigamo bhavati ‖*)

It is the custom to call any purifying thing or deity by the word pavitra. In this sense the deities Agni, Vāyu, Soma, Sūrya and Indra are also denoted by the word pavitra. The Vedic passage quoted above is the example on this point.

देवानामपि यंति पाथः (*devānāmapi yaṃti pāthaḥ*)—these waters enter the path of the gods' travel, that is, the atmosphere. An explanation is needed of what is meant by the waters entering the atmosphere. Reservoirs such as the sea, heated by the fierce rays of the sun, are transformed as vapour into clouds. The place of these clouds is the atmosphere. Therefore "the waters entered the atmosphere" means that the waters, in the form of clouds, pervade the atmosphere.

The same matter is supported by the words याः सूर्यो रश्मिभिः ततान (*yāḥ sūryo raśmibhiḥ tatāna*) of the next Ṛk.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> याः सूर्यो॑ र॒श्मिभि॑रात॒तान॒ याभ्य॒ इंद्रो॒ अर॑दद्गा॒तु-मू॒र्मिं ।  
> ते सिं॑धवो॒ वरि॑वो धातना नो यू॒यं पा॑त स्व॒स्तिभिः॒ सदा॑  
> नः ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> याः । सूर्यः । रश्मीभिः । आततान । याभ्यः । इंद्रः । अरदत् । गातुं । ऊर्मिं ।  
> ते । सिंधवः । वरिवः । धातन । नः । यूयं । पात । स्वस्तिभिः । सदा । नः ॥ ४ ॥

### Printed p. 284

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सूर्यो देवो या आपो रश्मिभिः स्वकीयैः किरणैराततान विस्तारयति । सूर्यो हि रश्मिभिरुदकसारमादाय वर्षतीत्यर्थः । याभ्योऽद्भ्य ऊर्मिं अर्तेरिदं रूपं । गमनयोग्यं गातुं मेघेभ्यो निर्गमनसाधनं मार्गमिंद्रोऽरदत् वज्रेण मेघांस्ताडयन्संयच्छति हे सिंधव आपः ते यूयं नोऽस्मभ्यं वरिवो धनं धातन धत्त । प्रयच्छत ॥ त इति सिंधुशब्देन समानाधिकरणत्वात्पुल्लिंगत्वं ॥ हे अब्देवताः यूयं सर्वदा नोऽस्मान्कल्याणैः पालयत ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The waters which the sun, the deity, spreads out with his own rays: the sun takes the essence of water with his rays and rains. For which waters Indra, with his thunderbolt, struck the clouds and made गातुं (*gātuṃ*), a path of issue from the clouds, and ऊर्मिं (*ūrmiṃ*) (a form of the root *arteḥ*), fit for going. "O rivers, waters, as such bestow वरिवः (*varivaḥ*), wealth, upon us." The word ते (*te*) is masculine through agreement with the word सिंधु (*siṃdhu*). "O deities of the waters, ever protect us with blessings."

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सिंधवः (*siṃdhavaḥ*)—O flowing ones
- याः (*yāḥ*)—whom
- सूर्यः (*sūryaḥ*)—the sun
- रश्मिभिः (*raśmibhiḥ*)—with his rays
- आततान (*ātatāna*)—made to spread
- याभ्यः (*yābhyaḥ*)—through whom
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- ऊर्मिं (*ūrmiṃ*)—the means of going
- गातुं (*gātuṃ*)—the path fit for the clouds to travel
- अरदत् (*aradat*)—made (with the help of the thunderbolt)
- ते (*te*)—such as you
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- वरिवः (*varivaḥ*)—wealth
- धातन (*dhātana*)—bestow
- यूयं (*yūyaṃ*)—you (O deities)
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- सदा (*sadā*)—always
- स्वस्तिभिः (*svastibhiḥ*)—with blessings
- पात (*pāta*)—protect

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O waters of flowing nature, you whom the sun has made to spread with his rays, through whom Indra, with the help of the thunderbolt, made a path serving as a means of going and fit for the travel of the clouds: as such, give us wealth. O deities, always protect us with blessings.

##### English translation
Waters, whom the sun has evaporized by his rays, for whom Indra has opened a path by which to issue, bestow upon us wealth : and do you (also) ever cherish us with blessings.

## Sūkta 48 (Maṇḍala 7)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ऋभुक्षण इति चतुरृचं पंचदशं सूक्तं वसिष्ठस्यार्षं त्रैष्टुभमृभुदेवताकं । अंत्याया विकल्पेन विश्वे देवा देवता । तथा चानुक्रांतं । ऋभुक्षण आर्भवमंत्या वैश्वदेवी वेति । दशमेऽहनि वैश्वदेवशस्त्रे आर्भवनिविद्धानं । सूत्रितं हि ऋभुक्षण इत्यार्भवं । आ. ८-१२ ॥ इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta ऋभुक्षणः (*ṛbhukṣaṇaḥ*) has four Ṛks and is the fifteenth sūkta; its seer is Vasiṣṭha, its metre is Triṣṭubh and its deity is the Ṛbhus. For the last verse the deity is, alternatively, the Viśve Devāḥ. The Anukramaṇī says: ऋभुक्षण आर्भवमंत्या वैश्वदेवी वेति (*ṛbhukṣaṇa ārbhavamaṃtyā vaiśvadevī veti*). On the tenth day, in the Vaiśvadeva śastra, the Ārbhava nividdhāna is used. It is stated in the sūtra: ऋभुक्षण इत्यार्भवं (*ṛbhukṣaṇa ityārbhavaṃ*) (Ā. 8-12).

### Printed p. 285
Anuvāda: This sūkta ऋभुक्षणः (*ṛbhukṣaṇaḥ*) is the fifteenth sūkta in the third anuvāka. It has four Ṛks. Its seer is Vasiṣṭha; the deity is the Ṛbhus, and for the last Ṛk the Ṛbhus or the Viśvedevas. In the Anukramaṇī it is said: ऋभुक्षण आर्भवॆ मंत्रा वैश्वदेवी वा (*ṛbhukṣaṇa ārbhavè maṃtrā vaiśvadevī vā*). When the Vaiśvadeva śastra mantras are recited on the tenth day of the sacrifice, this sūkta is to be added and recited as the nividdhānīya sūkta addressed to the Ṛbhus; this is set out by the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra, ऋभुक्षण इत्यार्भवं (*ṛbhukṣaṇa ityārbhavaṃ*) (Ā. 8-12).

Sūkta 48
Maṇḍala 7; Anuvāka 3; Sūkta 48.
Aṣṭaka 5; Adhyāya 4; Varga 15.
Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 4.
Seer: Vasiṣṭha.
Deity: 1–3 the Ṛbhus; 4 the Ṛbhus or the Viśve Devāḥ.
Metre: Triṣṭubh.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ऋभु॑क्षणो वाजा मा॒दय॑ध्वम॒स्मे न॑रो मघवानः सु॒तस्य॑ ।  
> आ वो॒ऽर्वाचः॒ क्रत॑वो॒ न या॒तां विभ्वो॒ रथं॒ नर्यं॑ वर्तयंतु ॥१॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ऋभुक्षणः । वाजाः । मादयध्वं । अस्मे इति । नरः । मघवानः । सुतस्य ।  
> आ । वः । अर्वाचः । क्रतवः । न । यातां । विभ्वः । रथं । नर्यं । वर्तयंतु ॥१॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ऋभुक्षेत्यृभूणां ज्येष्ठस्याख्या वाज इति तु कनिष्ठस्य । अत्रर्भुक्षणो वाजा इति बहुवचनेनर्भवस्त्रयो गृह्यंते । हे ऋभुक्षणो वाजा नरो नेतारो मघवानो धनवंत एवंभूता हे ऋभवः यूयमस्मे अस्मासु स्थितेन सुतस्याभिषुतेन सोमेन मादयध्वं । तृप्ता भवत । नेति संप्रत्यर्थे । इदानीं यातां गच्छतां वो युष्मदीयाः क्रतवः कर्मणां कर्तारो विभ्वो विभवः समर्था अश्वा आर्वाचोऽर्वांचोऽस्मदभिमुखा नर्यं मनुष्यहितं रथं युष्मदीयमा वर्तयंतु । आगमयंतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

ऋभुक्षा (*ṛbhukṣā*) is the name of the eldest of the Ṛbhus, वाज (*vāja*) of the youngest. Here, by the plural ऋभुक्षणो वाजा (*ṛbhukṣaṇo vājā*), the three Ṛbhus are meant. "O Ṛbhukṣans, Vājas, leaders, possessors of wealth, be gladdened (be satisfied) by the Soma pressed and standing with us." न (*na*) is in the sense of "now". "Now, as you go, may your क्रतवः (*kratavaḥ*) (doers of works), विभ्वः (*vibhvaḥ*) (powerful horses), turned towards us, bring your chariot, नर्यं (*naryaṃ*) (beneficial to men), hither."

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वाजाः (*vājāḥ*)—O possessors of food
- ऋभुक्षणः (*ṛbhukṣaṇaḥ*)—O Ṛbhus
- नरः (*naraḥ*)—leaders of rites
- मघवानः (*maghavānaḥ*)—possessors of wealth, you
- अस्मे (*asme*)—with that which is in us and
- सुतस्य (*sutasya*)—pressed, the Soma drink

### Printed p. 286
- मादयध्वं (*mādayadhvaṃ*)—be satisfied
- न (*na*)—now
- यातां (*yātāṃ*)—going
- वः (*vaḥ*)—your
- क्रतवः (*kratavaḥ*)—accomplishers of works
- विभ्वः (*vibhvaḥ*)—powerful horses
- अर्वाचः (*arvācaḥ*)—turned towards us
- नर्यं (*naryaṃ*)—beneficial to men
- रथं (*rathaṃ*)—your chariot
- आ वर्तयंतु (*ā vartayaṃtu*)—may they make come to us

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Ṛbhus, possessors of food, leaders of rites and possessors of wealth, be satisfied with the pressed Soma drink that is with us. May your powerful horses, accomplishers of works, now going and turned towards us, bring your chariot, beneficial to men, to us.

##### English translation
Ribhu, (Vibhu), and Vaja, leaders of rites, possessors of opulence, be exhilarated by our effused (libation) : may your active and powerful (horses) bring to our presence your chariot, beneficial to mankind.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this sūkta the deities called Ṛbhus are praised. We have explained these Ṛbhus many times before. It is well known that the three, Ṛbhu, Vibhu and Vāja, sons of the seer Sudhanvan, attained divinity through their good deeds and through helping the gods. They were pupils of Tvaṣṭṛ and skilled in the arts and crafts. It is said in various places that they made the thunderbolt for Indra and the chariot for the Aśvins. Their skill in craft is described: they made the single chamasa cup into four, and made their aged parents young. For explanations concerning them see Ṛ. part 3, page 4 and 3-16; 5-658; 8-666; 8-671, 8-693; 8-699; 8-704.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ऋ॒भुर्ऋ॒भुभि॑र॒भि वः॑ स्याम॒ विभ्वो॑ वि॒भुभिः॒ शव॑सा॒ शवां॑सि ।  
> वाजो॑ अ॒स्मां अ॑वतु॒ वाज॑साता॒विंद्रे॑ण यु॒जा त॑रुषेम वृ॒त्रं ॥२॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ऋभुः । ऋभुभिः । अभि । वः । स्याम । विभ्वः । विभुभिः । शवसा । शवांसि ।  
> वाजः । अस्मान् । अवतु । वाजसातौ । इंद्रेण । युजा । तरुषेम । वृत्रं ॥२॥

### Printed p. 287

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे ऋभवः ऋभुभिर्युष्माभिर्वयमृभुः । उरु भांतीत्यृभवः । संतो विभुभिर्युष्माभिर्विभ्वो विभवश्च संतः शवांसि शत्रूणां बलानि शवसा युष्मदीयेन बलेनाभि स्याम । अभिभवेम । तथा वाजसातौ संग्रामे वाज एतत्संज्ञक ऋभुरस्मानवतु । पालयतु । अपि च युजा सहायभूतेनेंद्रेण वृत्रं शत्रुं वयं तरुषेम । हनाम । प्रायेणर्भवोऽपींद्रेण सह स्तूयंत इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Ṛbhus, may we, being ऋभुः (*ṛbhuḥ*) with you the Ṛbhus (उरु भांतीत्यृभवः (*uru bhāṃtītyṛbhavaḥ*), those who shine widely), and विभ्वः (*vibhvaḥ*), mighty, with you the Vibhus, overcome the strengths of enemies with your strength. Likewise in battle may Vāja, the Ṛbhu of that name, protect us. And with Indra as our helper may we slay the enemy वृत्रं (*vṛtraṃ*)." Sāyaṇa adds that, as a rule, the Ṛbhus too are praised together with Indra.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Ṛbhus) वः ऋभुभिः (*vaḥ ṛbhubhiḥ*)—together with you, the shining ones
- ऋभुः (*ṛbhuḥ*)—possessing widespread radiance
- विभुभिः (*vibhubhiḥ*)—together with you, the wealthy ones
- विभ्वः (*vibhvaḥ*)—endowed with wealth and splendour
- शवांसि (*śavāṃsi*)—the strengths of enemies
- शवसा (*śavasā*)—with our strength
- अभि स्याम (*abhi syāma*)—may we defeat
- वाजसातौ (*vājasātau*)—in battle
- वाजः (*vājaḥ*)—Vāja among you
- अस्मान् (*asmān*)—us
- अवतु (*avatu*)—protect
- युजा (*yujā*)—who is our helper
- इंद्रेण (*iṃdreṇa*)—together with Indra
- वृत्रं (*vṛtraṃ*)—the obstructing enemy
- तरुषेम (*taruṣema*)—may we destroy

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Ṛbhus, joined with you the shining ones may we be possessed of widespread radiance, and joined with you the wealthy ones, endowed with wealth and splendour; may we defeat the strength of enemies with our strength. May Vāja, the mighty one among you, protect us. Together with Indra, our helper, may we destroy the obstructing enemy.

##### English translation
Mighty with the Ribhus, opulent with the Vibhus, may we overcome by strength the strength (of our foes): may Vaja defend us in battle : with Indra, our ally, may we destroy the enemy.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ते चि॒द्धि पू॒र्वीर॒भि संति॑ शा॒सा विश्वां॑ अ॒र्य उ॑प॒रता॑ति वन्वन् ।  
> इंद्रो॒ विभ्वां॑ ऋभु॒क्षा वाजो॑ अ॒र्यः शत्रो॑र्मिथ॒त्या कृ॑णव॒न्वि नृ॒म्णं ॥३॥

### Printed p. 288

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ते । चित् । हि । पूर्वीः । अभि । संति । शासा । विश्वान् । अर्यः । उपरताति । वन्वन् ।  
> इंद्रः । विभ्वा । ऋभुक्षाः । वाजः । अर्यः । शत्रोः । मिथत्या । कृणवन् । वि ।  
> नृम्णं ॥३॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ते तादृशा इंद्र ऋभवश्च पूर्वीर्बह्वीरस्मच्छत्रुसेनाः शासा शासनेन स्वकीययाज्ञया । यद्वा । विशस्यते हिंस्यतेऽनेनेति शासशब्द आयुधनाम । तेन । अभि संति । अभिभवंति । चिद्धीतीमौ पूरणौ । किंचोपरताति ॥ सप्तम्या लुक् ॥ उपरैरुपलैः पाषाणसदृशैरायुधैस्तायते विस्तार्यते इत्युपरताति युद्धं । तस्मिन्विश्वान् समस्तानर्योऽरींश्छत्रून्वन्वन् । हिंसंति । विभ्वा ऋभुक्षा वाज एतत्संज्ञका ऋभवश्चेंद्रश्चार्यः शत्रूणामभिगंतारः संतः शत्रोः संबंधि नृम्णं बलं मिथत्या ॥ मेथतेरिदं रूपं ॥ मिथतिर्हिंसा । तया वि कृणवन् । विकुर्वंतु । विनाशयंत्वित्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Those, Indra and the Ṛbhus, overcome the many hostile armies शासा (*śāsā*), by their command; or else शास (*śāsa*) is a name for a weapon (that by which one is struck), and they overcome with it. चित् (*cit*) and हि (*hi*) are expletives. उपरताति (*uparatāti*) (with elision of the locative ending) is battle, spread out with *uparaiḥ*, stone-like weapons; in it they slay all enemies. Vibhvan, Ṛbhukṣan and Vāja, and Indra, advancing on their enemies, वि कृणवन् (*vi kṛṇavan*), may they destroy the strength of the enemy मिथत्या (*mithatyā*) (a form of the root *methateḥ*; *mithatiḥ* is violence), by violence.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ते (*te*)—Indra and the Ṛbhus, possessed of the qualities mentioned
- पूर्वीः (*pūrvīḥ*)—the numerous enemy armies
- शासा (*śāsā*)—by their command
- अभि संति हि (*abhi saṃti hi*)—scorn and defeat
- उपरताति (*uparatāti*)—in battle
- विश्वान् (*viśvān*)—all
- आर्यः (*āryaḥ*)—enemies
- वन्वन् (*vanvan*)—they slay
- विभ्वा (*vibhvā*)—Vibhvan
- ऋभुक्षाः (*ṛbhukṣāḥ*)—Ṛbhukṣan
- वाजः (*vājaḥ*)—Vāja
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- आर्यः (*āryaḥ*)—surrounding the enemies
- शत्रोः (*śatroḥ*)—of the enemy
- नृम्णं (*nṛmṇaṃ*)—the strength
- मिथत्या (*mithatyā*)—by violence
- वि कृणवन् (*vi kṛṇavan*)—may they destroy

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> पूर्वोक्त गुणगळुळ्ळ इंद्र मत्तु ऋभुगळु अनेकसंख्यॆयुळ्ळ शत्रुसेनॆगळन्नु तम्म नियमनदिंद तिरस्करिसि सोलिसुत्तारॆ. युद्धदल्लि समस्त शत्रुगळन्नू नाशपडिसुत्तारॆ. विभ्वनू, ऋभुक्षनू, वाजनू, इंद्रनू शत्रुगळन्नु सुत्तुवरिदवरागि शत्रुविन बलवन्नु हिंसारूपदिंद नाशपडिसलि.

##### English translation
They verily, (Indra and the Ribhus), overcome multitudes by their prowess : they overcome all enemies in the missile conflict : may Indra, Vibwan Ribhukshin and Vaja, the subduers of foes, annihilate by their wrath the strength of the enemy.

### Printed p. 289

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> नू दे॑वासो॒ वरि॑वः कर्तना नो भू॒त नो॒ विश्वेऽव॑से स॒जोषाः॑ ।  
> सम॒स्मे इषं॒ वस॑वो ददीरन्यू॒यं पा॑त स्व॒स्तिभिः॒ सदा॑ नः ॥४॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> नु । देवासः । वरिवः । कर्तन । नः । भूत । नः । विश्वे । अवसे । सजोषाः ।  
> सं । अस्मे इति । इषं । वसवः । ददीरन् । यूयं । पात । स्वस्तिभिः । सदा । नः ॥४॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे देवासो देवा द्योतमाना ऋभवः यूयं न्वद्य नोऽस्मभ्यं वरिवो धनं कर्तन । कुरुत । प्रयच्छत । तथा विश्वे सर्व ऋभवो यूयं सजोषाः सह प्रीयमाणाः संतो नोऽस्माकमवसे रक्षणाय भूत । भवत । अपि च वसवः प्रशस्या ऋभव इषमन्नमस्मे अस्मभ्यं सं ददीरन् । सं प्रयच्छेयुः । हे ऋभवः यूयमस्मान् सर्वदा कल्याणै रक्षत ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O shining Ṛbhus, now grant us wealth (वरिवः (*varivaḥ*) means wealth); and all of you, being pleased together, be for our protection. Further, may the praiseworthy Ṛbhus (वसवः (*vasavaḥ*)) bestow food upon us. O Ṛbhus, protect us always with blessings.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- देवासः (*devāsaḥ*)—O Ṛbhus, divine in nature
- नु (*nu*)—now
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- वरिवः (*varivaḥ*)—wealth
- कर्तन (*kartana*)—provide
- विश्वे (*viśve*)—all of you
- सजोषाः (*sajoṣāḥ*)—being together delighted
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- अवसे (*avase*)—for protection
- भूत (*bhūta*)—resolve to be
- वसवः (*vasavaḥ*)—the praiseworthy Ṛbhus
- इषं (*iṣaṃ*)—food
- अस्मे (*asme*)—to us
- सं ददीरन् (*saṃ dadīran*)—may they provide and give
- (O gods) यूयं (*yūyaṃ*)—you
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- सदा (*sadā*)—always
- स्वस्तिभिः (*svastibhiḥ*)—with blessings
- पात (*pāta*)—protect

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Ṛbhus, divine in nature, provide us with wealth. May all of you be together delighted. Be our protectors. May the praiseworthy Ṛbhus provide us with food. O gods, protect us always with blessings.

##### English translation
Grant us, deities, this day opulence : may you all, well pleased alike, be (ready) for our protection : may the exalted (Ribhus) bestow upon us food : and do you (all) ever cherish us with blessings.

### Printed p. 290
## Sūkta 49 (Maṇḍala 7)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> समुद्रज्येष्ठा इति चतुर्ऋचं षोडशं सूक्तं वसिष्ठस्यार्षं त्रैष्टुभमब्देवताकं । तथा चानुक्रांतं । समुद्रज्येष्ठा आपमिति ॥ गतो विनियोगः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta समुद्रज्येष्ठाः (*samudrajyeṣṭhāḥ*) has four Ṛks and is the sixteenth sūkta. Its seer is Vasiṣṭha, its deity the Waters, its metre Triṣṭubh. The Anukramaṇī says: समुद्रज्येष्ठा आपमिति (*samudrajyeṣṭhā āpamiti*). The viniyoga has already been stated.

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta beginning समुद्रज्येष्ठाः (*samudrajyeṣṭhāḥ*) is the sixteenth sūkta in the third anuvāka. It has four Ṛks. For this sūkta Vasiṣṭha is the seer; Āpaḥ (the waters) is the deity; the metre is Triṣṭubh. In the Anukramaṇī it is said: समुद्रज्येष्ठा आपं (*samudrajyeṣṭhā āpaṃ*). The viniyoga was stated earlier.

Sūkta—49
Maṇḍala 7; Anuvāka 3; Sūkta 49.
Aṣṭaka 5; Adhyāya 4; Varga 16.
Number of Ṛks in the sūkta—4.
Seer: Vasiṣṭha.
Deity: Āpaḥ.
Metre: Triṣṭubh.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स॒मु॒द्रज्ये॑ष्ठाः सलि॒लस्य॒ मध्या॑त्पुना॒ना यं॒त्यनि॑विशमानाः ।  
> इंद्रो॒ या व॒ज्री वृ॑ष॒भो र॒राद॒ ता आपो॑ दे॒वीरि॒ह माम॑वंतु ॥१॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> समुद्रज्येष्ठाः । सलिलस्य । मध्यात् । पुनानाः । यंति । अनिविशमानाः ।  
> इंद्रः । याः । वज्री । वृषभः । रराद । ताः । आपः । देवीः । इह । मां । अवन् ॥१॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> समुद्रज्येष्ठाः समुद्रोर्णवो ज्येष्ठः प्रशस्यतमो यासामपां ताः । सलिलस्य । अंतरिक्षनामैतत् । अंतरिक्षस्य मध्यान्माध्यमिकात्स्थानाद्यंति । गच्छंति । कीदृश्यः । पुनाना विश्वं शोधयंत्योऽनिविशमानाः सर्वदा गच्छंत्यः । वज्री वज्रभृद्वृषभः कामानां वर्षितेंद्रो या निरुद्धा अपो रराद लिखति देवीर्देव्यस्ता आप इहास्मिन्प्रदेशे स्थितं मामवंतु । रक्षंतु । अभि गच्छंतु वा ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

समुद्रज्येष्ठाः (*samudrajyeṣṭhāḥ*): the waters whose chief, most praiseworthy one is the sea. सलिलस्य (*salilasya*) is a name of the atmosphere (antarikṣa); the waters come from the middle, the middle region of the atmosphere. They go, purifying the world (पुनानाः (*punānāḥ*)), not stopping (अनिविशमानाः (*aniviśamānāḥ*)), flowing always. The thunderbolt-bearing Indra, the showerer of desires, tore open (रराद (*rarāda*), "cuts") the waters that had been confined. May those divine waters protect me who stand here in this place, or come to me.

### Printed p. 291

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पुनानाः (*punānāḥ*)—purifying the world
- अनिविशमानाः (*aniviśamānāḥ*)—flowing without interruption
- समुद्रज्येष्ठाः (*samudrajyeṣṭhāḥ*)—the waters whose best resting-place is the sea
- सलिलस्य (*salilasya*)—of the atmosphere
- मध्यात् (*madhyāt*)—from the middle
- यंति (*yaṃti*)—flow down
- वज्री (*vajrī*)—the bearer of the thunderbolt
- वृषभः (*vṛṣabhaḥ*)—the showerer of desires
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- याः (*yāḥ*)—which waters
- रराद (*rarāda*)—he split (the obstruction) and made them flow
- देवीः (*devīḥ*)—divine
- ताः आपः (*tāḥ āpaḥ*)—those same waters (the presiding deities of the waters)
- इह (*iha*)—here, engaged in this sacrifice
- मां (*māṃ*)—me
- अवंतु (*avaṃtu*)—may they protect

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The waters, which purify the world, flow without interruption and have the sea as their best resting-place, come flowing from the middle of the atmosphere. May those same divine waters (the presiding deities of the waters), which Indra, the bearer of the thunderbolt and showerer of desires, released from bondage and made to flow, protect me, engaged in this sacrifice.

##### English translation
The waters, with their ocean-chief, proceed from the midst of the firmament, purifying (all things), flowing unceasingly: may the divine waters, whom the thunder-bearing Indra, the showerer, sent forth, protect me here (on earth).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

समुद्रज्येष्ठाः (*samudrajyeṣṭhāḥ*) means waters that have the sea as their best resting-place. Here another matter must be explained. All the waters on the earth have the sea as their place of origin. That is, the waters in lakes, ponds, wells, pools, rivers and so on were at one time part of the sea. The sea water, heated by the sun's rays, is converted into vapour and becomes cloud-masses. Afterwards, of the waters those clouds pour down in rain, the greater part flows as rivers and again joins the sea. Some part stays in reservoirs such as lakes, pools and tanks. A little part sinks into the earth, travels a short distance, and after meeting a hard rock is stored there. As this water has no contact with the sun's rays, there is no chance of its evaporating. Therefore, since part of the rain-water that falls every year in the rainy season sinks into the earth and is stored, its quantity remains large. The water that wells up when we dig a well is of this kind. This water, and the water in lakes, ponds and rivers, is not salty like sea water but clear and sweet. For the water that evaporates from the sea does not carry away the salt in it but leaves it there; only clear water goes off as vapour. Therefore these waters are sweet, not salty. The reason why sea water is salty is this: when the earth was first created, oxygen (Oxegen) and hydrogen (Hydrogen), which were in the form of vapour, combined and water arose. Then it was clear, without any colour, taste or smell. All that water filled the low-lying regions of the earth. This is the sea. Gradually, at every moment, the sea water is heated by the sun's rays, some part goes off as vapour, stays for some time as cloud in the atmosphere, falls on the earth as rain, flows as rivers and joins the sea. When the rain-water flows as a river it carries away the soil and other mineral substances on the earth and joins the sea. Although the soil and such things remain with the water for some time, after the water joins the sea that soil settles on the sea-bed; only the clear water mixes with the sea water. But as a river flows, other minerals, and chiefly salt (Chloride), are well dissolved in the water, so that even after the river joins the sea this salt does not separate from the water. Instead it makes the sea water salty. In this way thousands of rivers every year bring a little salt to the sea. Since this work has gone on without interruption for crores of years, the sea water is as very salty as it now is. Every year more and more salt reaches the sea through the rivers, but that salt cannot in any way leave the sea. Therefore the sea water gradually becomes more and more salty.

### Printed p. 292
Reckoning from the salt content of sea water and from the amount of salt that reaches the sea each year, scientists can find out how many crores of years have passed since the earth came into being.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> या आपो॑ दि॒व्या उ॒त वा॒ स्रवं॑ति ख॒नित्रि॑मा उ॒त वा॒ याः स्व॑यं॒जाः ।  
> स॒मु॒द्रार्था॒ याः शुच॑यः पाव॒कास्ता आपो॑ दे॒वीरि॒ह माम॑वंतु ॥२॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> याः । आपः । दिव्याः । उत । वा । स्रवंति । खनित्रिमाः । उत । वा । याः । स्वयंजाः ।  
> समुद्रऽअर्थाः । याः । शुचयः । पावकाः । ताः । आपः । देवीः । इह । मां ।  
> अवंतु ॥२॥

### Printed p. 293

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> या आपो दिव्या अंतरिक्षभवाः संति । उत वापि च या आपो नद्यादिगताः सत्यः स्रवंति गच्छंति । याश्च खनित्रिमाः खननेन निर्वृत्ताः । उत वापि च याः स्वयंजाः स्वयमेव प्रादुर्भवंत्यः समुद्रार्थाः । समुद्र एवार्थो गंतव्यो यासां ताः समुद्रार्थाः । शुचयो दीप्तियुक्ताः पावकाः शोधयित्र्यश्च भवंति । ता आपो मामवंत्विति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The waters that are दिव्याः (*divyāḥ*), born in the atmosphere; those that, having reached rivers and the like, flow; those that are खनित्रिमाः (*khanitrimāḥ*), produced by digging; and those that are स्वयंजाः (*svayaṃjāḥ*), appearing of themselves; those समुद्रार्थाः (*samudrārthāḥ*), whose goal to be reached is the sea alone; those that are शुचयः (*śucayaḥ*), bright, and पावकाः (*pāvakāḥ*), purifying: may those waters protect me.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- याः आपः (*yāḥ āpaḥ*)—which waters
- दिव्याः (*divyāḥ*)—are born in the heavenly world
- उत वा (*uta vā*)—and
- स्रवंति (*sravaṃti*)—are in the form of streams
- उत वा (*uta vā*)—besides
- याः (*yāḥ*)—which waters
- खनित्रिमाः (*khanitrimāḥ*)—flow out by splitting an obstruction
- उत वा (*uta vā*)—and moreover
- याः (*yāḥ*)—which waters
- स्वयंजाः (*svayaṃjāḥ*)—appear of themselves
- समुद्रार्थाः (*samudrārthāḥ*)—go and join the sea
- देवीः (*devīḥ*)—divine
- ता आपः (*tā āpaḥ*)—those same waters
- इह (*iha*)—here, engaged in this sacrifice
- मां (*māṃ*)—me
- अवंतु (*avaṃtu*)—may they protect

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The waters that are born in the heavenly world, those that are in the form of streams, those that flow out by splitting their obstruction, and those that appear of themselves and go and join the sea: may those same divine waters protect me, engaged in this sacrifice.

##### English translation
May the waters that are in the sky, or those that flow (on the earth), those (whose channels) have been dug, or those that have sprung up spontaneously, and those that seek the ocean, all pnre and purifying, may those divine waters protect me here (on earth).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

या आपः दिव्याः (*yā āpaḥ divyāḥ*)—waters born in the heavenly world or the atmosphere, that is, rain-water falling as rain from the cloud-masses.
उत वा स्रवंति (*uta vā sravaṃti*)—flowing water, that is, river water flowing as a stream.
याः खनित्रिमाः (*yāḥ khanitrimāḥ*)—the water of reservoirs such as wells and pools, dug with digging tools such as spades.
याः स्वयंजाः (*yāḥ svayaṃjāḥ*)—water that collects beneath the earth and wells up through springs and the like.

### Printed p. 294
#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यासां॒ राजा॒ वरु॑णो॒ याति॒ मध्ये॑ सत्यानृ॒ते अ॑व॒पश्य॒ञ्जना॑नां ।  
> म॒धु॒श्चुतः॒ शुच॑यो॒ याः पा॑व॒कास्ता आपो॑ दे॒वीरि॒ह माम॑वंतु ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यासां । राजा । वरुणः । याति । मध्ये । सत्यानृते । इति । अवपश्यन् । जनानां ।  
> मधुश्चुतः । शुचयः । याः । पावकाः । ताः । आपः । देवीः । इह । मां । अवंतु ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> वरुणो यासामपां राजा स्वामी मध्ये मध्यमलोके याति गच्छति । किं कुर्वन् । जनानां प्रजानां सत्यानृते सत्यं चानृतं चावपश्यन् । जानन्नित्यर्थः । या आपो मधुश्चुतो रसं स्रवंत्यः शुचयो दीप्तियुक्ताः पावकाः शोधयित्र्यः ता आपो देव्यो मां रक्षंत्विति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Varuna, the king, the lord, of whatever waters, goes in the middle, in the middle world, seeing (अवपश्यन् (*avapaśyan*), that is, knowing) the truth and falsehood of the people. The waters that shed sweetness (मधुश्चुतः (*madhuścutaḥ*), flowing with sap), bright (शुचयः (*śucayaḥ*)) and purifying (पावकाः (*pāvakāḥ*)): may those divine waters protect me.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मध्ये (*madhye*)—stationed in the middle world
- वरुणः (*varuṇaḥ*)—the god Varuna
- जनानां (*janānāṃ*)—of the people
- सत्यानृते (*satyānṛte*)—the true and untrue lives
- अवपश्यन् (*avapaśyan*)—seeing
- यासां (*yāsāṃ*)—of which waters
- राजा (*rājā*)—as lord
- याति (*yāti*)—he moves
- याः (*yāḥ*)—which waters
- मधुश्चुतः (*madhuścutaḥ*)—squeezing out sweet juices
- शुचयः (*śucayaḥ*)—shining
- पावकाः (*pāvakāḥ*)—purifying
- देवीः (*devīḥ*)—divine
- ताः आपः (*tāḥ āpaḥ*)—those same waters
- इह (*iha*)—here, engaged in this sacrifice
- मां (*māṃ*)—me
- अवंतु (*avaṃtu*)—may they protect

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The god Varuna, stationed in the middle world, moves as lord of those waters, watching the true and untrue lives of the people. May those same divine waters, which squeeze out sweet juices and are shining and purifying, protect me, engaged in this sacrifice.

##### English translation
Those whose sovereign, Varuna, passes in the middle sphere, discriminating the truth and falsehood of mankind : those shedding sweet showers, pure and purifying : may those divine waters protect me here (on earth).

### Printed p. 295

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

यासां राजा वरुणः (*yāsāṃ rājā varuṇaḥ*)—यासां उदकानां राजा वरुणः (*yāsāṃ udakānāṃ rājā varuṇaḥ*). "Varuṇa, king of the waters" is said here; this means Varuṇa, the deity presiding over the waters. There is also a Taittirīya Śruti sentence: वरुणोऽसामधिपतिः (*varuṇo 'sāmadhipatiḥ*). Moreover Varuṇa is well known as the deity presiding over night. मैत्रं वा अहः । वारुणी रात्रिः (*maitraṃ vā ahaḥ | vāruṇī rātriḥ*): that is, the Taittirīya Śruti itself says that Mitra is the lord of day and Varuṇa of night.
राजा वरुणः (*rājā varuṇaḥ*)—Here Varuṇa is called by the word राजा (*rājā*). In many places Varuṇa, or Mitra and Varuṇa, are also called by the word सम्राज (*samrāja*). For it is said in many places that Varuṇa watches the deeds that people do, their truth and falsehood, and that all are subject to his ordinance. In the present Ṛk it is said सत्यानृते अव पश्यन् जनानां (*satyānṛte ava paśyan janānāṃ*). On the point that all are subject to Varuṇa's ordinances, the use of the word धृतव्रत (*dhṛtavrata*) for Varuṇa supports this.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यासु॒ राजा॒ वरु॑णो॒ यासु॒ सोमो॒ विश्वे॑दे॒वा यासूर्जं॒ मदं॑ति ।  
> वै॒श्वा॒न॒रो यास्व॒ग्निः प्रवि॑ष्ट॒स्ता आपो॑ दे॒वीरि॒ह माम॑वंतु ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यासु । राजा । वरुणः । यासु । सोमः । विश्वे । देवाः । यासु । ऊर्जं । मदंति ।  
> वैश्वानरः । यासु । अग्निः । प्रविष्टः । ताः । आपः । देवीः । इह । मां । अवंतु ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> आपां राजा वरुणो यास्वप्सु वर्तते । सोमो यास्वप्सु वर्तते । यास्वप्सु स्थिता विश्वे सर्वे देवा ऊर्जमन्नं मदंति । वैश्वानरोऽग्निर्यासु प्रविष्टः । ता आपो देव्य इह स्थितं मामवंतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Varuṇa, king of the waters, abides in which waters; Soma abides in which waters; the All-gods, abiding in which waters, delight in food (ऊर्जं (*ūrjaṃ*)); Vaiśvānara Agni has entered which waters. May those divine waters protect me, who stand here.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यासु (*yāsu*)—in which waters
- राजा (*rājā*)—the sovereign
- वरुणः (*varuṇaḥ*)—Varuṇa shines
- यासु (*yāsu*)—in which waters
- सोमः (*somaḥ*)—Soma abides
- यासु (*yāsu*)—in which waters
- विश्वे देवाः (*viśve devāḥ*)—all the gods
- ऊर्जं (*ūrjaṃ*)—food
- मदंति (*madaṃti*)—relish
- यासु (*yāsu*)—in which waters
- वैश्वानरः (*vaiśvānaraḥ*)—leader of the whole world
- अग्निः (*agniḥ*)—Agni

### Printed p. 296
- प्रविष्टः (*praviṣṭaḥ*)—entered and lay hidden
- देवीः (*devīḥ*)—divine in nature
- ताः आपः (*tāḥ āpaḥ*)—those same waters
- इह (*iha*)—engaged in this sacrifice
- मां (*māṃ*)—me
- अवंतु (*avaṃtu*)—may they protect

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

In which waters the sovereign Varuṇa shines, in which waters Soma abides, in which waters all the gods relish food, in which waters Agni in the form of Vaiśvānara entered: may those same waters, divine in nature, protect me, who am engaged in this sacrifice.

##### English translation
May they in which their king, Varuna, in which Soma, abides, in which the gods delight (to receive) the sacrificial food, into which Agni Vaishwanara entered, may those divine waters protect me here (on earth).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

वैश्वानरो यासु अग्निः प्रविष्टः (*vaiśvānaro yāsu agniḥ praviṣṭaḥ*)—The prayer is stated here that the waters in which Agni, in the form of Vaiśvānara, entered and lay hidden may protect me. As to why Agni hid in the water, an ancient story is told in the Ṛgveda and in the Yajurveda:
अग्नेः पूर्वे भ्रातरो अर्थमेतं रथीवाध्वानमन्वावरीवुः । तस्माद्भिया वरुण दूरमायं गौरो न क्षेप्नोरविजे ज्यायाः ॥ कुर्मस्त आयुरजरं यदग्ने यथा युक्तो जातवेदो न रिष्याः । अथा वहासि सुमनस्यमानो भागं देवेभ्यो हविषः सुजात ॥ प्रयाजान्मे अनुयाजांश्च केवलानूर्जस्वंतं हविषो दत्त भागं । घृतं चापां पुरुषं चौषधीनामग्नेश्च दीर्घमायुरस्तु देवाः । तव प्रयाजा अनुयाजाश्च केवल ऊर्जस्वंतो हविषः संतु भागाः । तवाग्ने यज्ञोऽयमस्तु सर्वस्तुभ्यं नमंतां प्रदिशश्चतस्रः ॥ (*agneḥ pūrve bhrātaro arthametaṃ rathīvādhvānamanvāvarīvuḥ | tasmādbhiyā varuṇa dūramāyaṃ gauro na kṣepnoravije jyāyāḥ ‖ kurmasta āyurajaraṃ yadagne yathā yukto jātavedo na riṣyāḥ | athā vahāsi sumanasyamāno bhāgaṃ devebhyo haviṣaḥ sujāta ‖ prayājānme anuyājāṃśca kevalānūrjasvaṃtaṃ haviṣo datta bhāgaṃ | ghṛtaṃ cāpāṃ puruṣaṃ cauṣadhīnāmagneśca dīrghamāyurastu devāḥ | tava prayājā anuyājāśca kevala ūrjasvaṃto haviṣaḥ saṃtu bhāgāḥ | tavāgne yajño 'yamastu sarvastubhyaṃ namaṃtāṃ pradiśaścatasraḥ ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-51-6 ff.)
In this sūkta a conversation between the gods and Agni is related. The main point is this: Agni had three elder brothers named Bhūpati, Bhuvanapati and Bhūtānāṃ Pati. Like Agni they carried to the gods the offering that the sacrificer makes in the sacrifice. While matters stood so, these three brothers died through the offerings made with the vajra-like call वषट्कार (*vaṣaṭkāra*). Seeing it, Agni, the youngest, fearing the same fate for himself, went and hid in the water. While the gods searched for Agni without finding him, the fish in the water told the gods that Agni was in the water. Then the gods

### Printed p. 297
