came to Agni and spoke with him thus. This conversation is what is set out in this sūkta. When the gods begged Agni to rise from the water and carry the offering to them as before, Agni said that since his brothers had died the same end would befall him, so he would not come. The gods replied that he should not fear death, and that they would see that he had no fear of death. Agni then said: very well; besides sparing me the fear of death, you must give me certain special offerings in the sacrifices: prayāja, anuyāja, ūrjasvat, sviṣṭakṛt, ājyabhāga and so on. The gods agreed, gave him the shares of the offering he asked for, granted the boons that he should play the chief part in the sacrifice, be saluted by all and be long-lived, and appointed him as before to the work of carrying the offering. This is the main purport of this sūkta. In the Taittirīya Saṃhitā of the Yajurveda it is as follows:
अग्नेस्त्रयो ज्यायाग्ंसो भ्रातर आसंते देवेभ्यो हव्यं वहंतः प्रामीयंत सोऽग्निरबिभेदित्थं वाव स्य आर्तिमारिष्यतीति स निलायत सोऽपः प्राविशत्तं देवताः प्रैषमैच्छन्तं मत्स्यः प्राब्रवीत्तमशपद्धियाधियात्वा वध्यासुर्यो मा प्रावोच इति तस्मान्मत्स्यं धियाधिया घ्नंति शप्तो हि तमब्रुवन्नुप न आ वर्तस्व हव्यं नो वहेति सोऽब्रवीद्वरं वृणै यदेव गृहीतस्याहुतस्य बहिः परिधिस्कंदात्तन्मे भ्रातृणां भागधेयमसदिति तस्माद्यद्गृहीतस्याहुतस्य बहिः परिधि स्कंदति तेषां तद्भागधेयं तानेव तेन प्रीणाति परिधीन्परि दधाति रक्षसामसहत्यै ॥ (*agnestrayo jyāyāgṃso bhrātara āsaṃte devebhyo havyaṃ vahaṃtaḥ prāmīyaṃta so 'gnirabibheditthaṃ vāva sya ārtimāriṣyatīti sa nilāyata so 'paḥ prāviśattaṃ devatāḥ praiṣamaicchantaṃ matsyaḥ prābravīttamaśapaddhiyādhiyātvā vadhyāsuryo mā prāvoca iti tasmānmatsyaṃ dhiyādhiyā ghnaṃti śapto hi tamabruvannupa na ā vartasva havyaṃ no vaheti so 'bravīdvaraṃ vṛṇai yadeva gṛhītasyāhutasya bahiḥ paridhiskaṃdāttanme bhrātṛṇāṃ bhāgadheyamasaditi tasmādyadgṛhītasyāhutasya bahiḥ paridhi skaṃdati teṣāṃ tadbhāgadheyaṃ tāneva tena prīṇāti paridhīnpari dadhāti rakṣasāmasahatyai ‖*)
(Tai. Saṃ. 2-6-6-1, 2)
Formerly Agni had three elder brothers. They carried to the gods the offering that the sacrificer makes in sacrifices. When they died, Agni feared the same fate might befall him, ran away, entered the water and hid so that no one saw him. While the gods searched for where Agni had gone, a fish in the water told the gods that Agni was hiding in the water. Hearing this tale-bearing of the fish, Agni grew angry and cursed that henceforth people should catch and kill the fish in the water with nets and other means. Accordingly people even now catch, kill and eat fish. Then the gods said to Agni: "O Agni, come back and be ready for the work of carrying the offering for us." Agni said that if they granted him the boon that the portion of the offerings, such as ājya, that falls outside the paridhi (paridhi is the name of the special darbha-stalks laid on the four sides of the fire-pit so that demons may not come and obstruct the sacrifice) when the offering is made in the sacrifice, should be a share of the offering for his brothers, he would rise from the water and carry the offering as before. The gods agreed and granted him that boon. Such is the narrative.

### Printed p. 298
Sūkta 50 follows.

## Sūkta 50 (Maṇḍala 7)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> आ मां मित्रावरुणेति चतुर्ऋचं सप्तदशं सूक्तं वसिष्ठस्यार्षं । चतुर्थ्यतिजगती व्यूहेन शक्वरी वादितस्तिस्रो जगत्यः । प्रथमा मैत्रावरुणी द्वितीयाग्नेयी तृतीया वैश्वदेवी चतुर्थी गंगादिनदीदेवताका । तथा चानुक्रमणिका । आ मां मैत्रावरुण्याग्नेयी वैश्वदेवी नदीस्तुतिर्जागतमंत्यातिजगती शक्वरी वेति ॥ अस्य सूक्तस्य प्रत्यृचं विषादिहरणे विनियोगो लिंगादवगंतव्यः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta आ मां मित्रावरुणेति (*ā māṃ mitrāvaruṇeti*) has four Ṛks and is the seventeenth sūkta; Vasiṣṭha is the seer. The fourth Ṛk is Atijagatī, or Śakvarī by vyūha; the first three are Jagatī. The first Ṛk is addressed to Mitra and Varuṇa, the second to Agni, the third to the All-gods, the fourth to rivers such as the Gaṅgā. He cites the Anukramaṇikā: आ मां मैत्रावरुण्याग्नेयी वैश्वदेवी नदीस्तुतिर्जागतमंत्यातिजगती शक्वरी वेति (*ā māṃ maitrāvaruṇyāgneyī vaiśvadevī nadīstutirjāgatamaṃtyātijagatī śakvarī veti*). The use of each Ṛk of this sūkta, in removing dejection and the like, is to be understood from the sense (लिंग (*liṃga*)).

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta, आ मां मित्रावरुणा (*ā māṃ mitrāvaruṇā*), is the seventeenth sūkta in the third anuvāka. It has four Ṛks. Vasiṣṭha is the seer; the first three Ṛks are in the Jagatī metre; the fourth is Atijagatī or Śakvarī. The deity of the first Ṛk is Mitra and Varuṇa; of the second, Agni; of the third, the All-gods; of the fourth, rivers such as the Gaṅgā. It is said: आ मां मैत्रावरुण्याग्नेयी वैश्वदेवी नदीस्तुतिर्जागतमंत्यातिजगती शक्वरी वा (*ā māṃ maitrāvaruṇyāgneyī vaiśvadevī nadīstutirjāgatamaṃtyātijagatī śakvarī vā*). The use of each Ṛk of this sūkta is to be understood as applying, according to the sense, to the removal of dejection and the like.

Sūkta 50
Maṇḍala 7 ‖ Anuvāka 3 ‖ Sūkta 50 ‖
Aṣṭaka 5 ‖ Adhyāya 4 ‖ Varga 14 ‖
Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 4 ‖
Ṛṣi: Vasiṣṭha ‖
Deity: 1. Mitra and Varuṇa. 2. Agni. 3. All-gods. 4. Rivers ‖
Metre: 1-3 Jagatī. 4. Atijagatī or Śakvarī ‖

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ मां मि॑त्रावरुणे॒ह र॑क्षतं कुला॒यय॑द्वि॒श्वय॒न्मा न॒ आ ग॑न् ।  
> अ॒ज॒का॒वं दु॒र्दृशी॑कं ति॒रो द॑धे॒ मा मां पद्ये॑न॒ रप॑सा विद॒त्सरुः॑ ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । मां । मित्रावरुणा । इह । रक्षतं । कुलाययत् । विश्वयत् । मा । नः । आ । गन् ।  
> अजकावं । दुःदृशीकं । तिरः । दधे । मा । मां । पद्येन । रपसा । विदत् । त्सरुः ॥ १ ॥

### Printed p. 299

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मित्रावरुणा हे मित्रावरुणौ युवामिहास्मिल्लोके मामा रक्षतं । आभिमुख्येन पालयतं । कुलाययत् । कुलायं स्थानं । तत्कुर्वद्विश्वयद्विशेषेण वर्धमानं विषं नोऽस्माना आभिमुख्येन मा गन् । मा गमत् । मा गच्छतु । तथाजकावं । अजका नाम रोगविशेषः । तद्वद्दुर्दृशीकं दुर्दर्शनं विषं तिरो दधे । तिरो धत्तां । नश्यत्वित्यर्थः । तथा त्सरुश्छद्मगामी । जिह्मगः सर्प इत्यर्थः । मां पद्येन पादभवेन रपसा । रपिः शब्दकर्मा । शब्देन मा विदत् । न जानातु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Mitra and Varuṇa, protect me here in this world, facing me. May the poison (विषं (*viṣaṃ*)) that makes a place for itself (कुलाययत् (*kulāyayat*), कुलायं (*kulāyaṃ*) meaning "place") and grows specially (विश्वयत् (*viśvayat*)) not come to us. May the poison that is like अजका (*ajakā*) (अजका (*ajakā*) is a particular disease) and ugly to look at disappear. And may the त्सरुः (*tsaruḥ*), the crooked-going one, that is the snake, not know me by the sound (रपसा (*rapasā*); रपिः (*rapiḥ*) means sound) arising from my foot.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मित्रावरुणा (*mitrāvaruṇā*)—O Mitra and Varuṇa
- इह (*iha*)—in this sacrifice
- मां (*māṃ*)—me
- आ रक्षतं (*ā rakṣataṃ*)—protect
- कुलाययत् (*kulāyayat*)—that which makes a place here
- विश्वयत् (*viśvayat*)—and thrives everywhere, the poison
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- मा आ गन् (*mā ā gan*)—let it not come near
- अजकावं (*ajakāvaṃ*)—that which causes disease
- दुर्दृशीकं (*durdṛśīkaṃ*)—and is loathsome to see, the poison
- तिरोदधे (*tirodadhe*)—let it vanish
- त्सरुः (*tsaruḥ*)—the crooked-going snake
- मां (*māṃ*)—me
- पद्येन (*padyena*)—arising from the movement of my foot
- रपसा (*rapasā*)—by the sound
- मा विदत् (*mā vidat*)—let it not know (my presence)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Mitra and Varuṇa, protect me in this sacrifice. May the poison that makes a place here and thrives everywhere not come to us and approach us. May the poison that causes disease and is loathsome to see vanish. May the crooked-going snake not know (my presence) by the sound arising from the movement of my foot.

##### English translation
Mitra and Varuna, protect me here (in this world): let not the insidious and spreading (poison) reach me : may the equally malignant and undiscernible (venom) disappear : let not the tortuous (snake) recognize me by the sound of my footsteps.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यद्वि॒जाम॒न्परु॑षि॒ वंद॑नं॒ भुव॑दष्ठी॒वंतौ॒ परि॑ कु॒ल्फौ च॒ देह॑त् ।  
> अ॒ग्निष्टच्छोच॒न्नप॑ बाधतामि॒तो मा मां पद्ये॑न॒ रप॑सा विद॒त्सरुः॑ ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यत् । वि5जामन् । परुषि । वंदनं । भुवत् । अस्थीवंतौ । परि । कुल्फौ । च । देहत् ।  
> अग्निः । तत् । शोचन् । अप । बाधतां । इतः । मां । मां । पद्येन । रपसा । विदत् । सरुः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> वंदनमेतत्संज्ञकं यद्विषं विजामन् विविधजन्मनि परुषि वृक्षादीनां पर्वणि भुवत् उद्भवेत् । यच्च विषमस्थीवंतौ जानुनी कुल्फौ गुल्फौ च परि देहत् ॥ दिह उपचये ॥ उपचितं कुर्यात् । अग्निर्देवः शोचन् दीप्यमानः सन्नितोऽस्माज्जनात्तद्विषमप बाधतां । अपहंतु । शिष्टं व्याख्यातं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The poison called वंदन (*vaṃdana*), which arises in many births (विजामन् (*vijāman*)), in the joints (परुषि (*paruṣi*)) of trees and the like, and the poison that accumulates (देहत् (*dehat*), from दिह (*diha*) in the sense of accumulating) in the knees (अस्थीवंतौ (*asthīvaṃtau*)) and ankles (कुल्फौ (*kulphau*) = गुल्फौ (*gulphau*)): may Agni, shining, drive that poison away from this person. The rest is as explained.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वंदनं (*vaṃdanaṃ*)—called vandana
- यत् (*yat*)—which poison
- विजामन् (*vijāman*)—in many forms
- परुषि (*paruṣi*)—in the joints of trees and the like
- भुवत् (*bhuvat*)—arises
- (यत् (*yat*)—which) अस्थीवंतौ (*asthīvaṃtau*)—in the knees
- कुल्फौ च (*kulphau ca*)—and in the joints of the feet
- परि देहत् (*pari dehat*)—fills
- तत् (*tat*)—that poison
- शोचन् (*śocan*)—shining
- अग्निः (*agniḥ*)—Agni
- इतः (*itaḥ*)—from our place
- अप बाधतां (*apa bādhatāṃ*)—let him drive far away and destroy
- सरुः (*saruḥ*)—the crooked-going snake
- मां (*māṃ*)—me
- पद्येन (*padyena*)—arising from the movement of my foot
- रपसा (*rapasā*)—by the sound
- मा विदत् (*mā vidat*)—let it not know (my presence)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The poison called vandana, which arises in many forms in the joints of trees and the like, and which fills the joints of the knees and the feet: may the shining Agni drive that poison far from our place and destroy it. May the crooked-going snake not know me by the sound arising from the movement of my foot.

##### English translation
May the brilliant Agni counteract that poison which is generated in the manifold knots (of trees) and the like, or which is smeared upon the knees or ankles: let not the tortuous snake recognize me by the sound of my footsteps.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यच्छ॑ल्म॒लौ भव॑ति॒ यन्न॒दीषु॒ यदोष॑धीभ्यः॒ परि॒ जाय॑ते  ॒
> विषं ।

### Printed p. 301

> विश्वे देवा निरितस्तत्सुवंतु मा मां पद्येन रपसा विदत्सरुः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यत् । शल्मलौ । भवति । यत् । नदीषु । यत् । ओषधीभ्यः । परि । जायते । विषं ।  
> विश्वे । देवाः । निः । इतः । तत् । सुवंतु । मा । मां । पद्येन । रपसा । विदत् ।  
> सरुः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यद्विषं शल्मलावेतत्संज्ञके वृक्षे भवति । यद्विषं नदीषु तत्रस्थास्वप्सु प्रादुर्भवति । परीति संचम्यर्थानुवादी । ओषधीभ्यः सकाशाद्विषं जायते उत्पद्यते च । विश्वे सर्वे देवास्तद्विषमितोऽस्माज्जनाद्देशाद्वा निः सुवंतु । निःशेषेण प्रेरयंतु । मा मामिति शिष्टं व्याख्यातं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The poison that arises in the tree called Śalmali, and the poison that appears in the rivers, that is, in the waters that stand in them, are named. The word परि (*pari*) is an equivalent of the fifth case ("from"), restating its sense: the poison also arises from plants. May all the gods drive that poison out from this person or this place; may they send it out completely. The rest, "let it not (find) me", has been explained already.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यत् (*yat*)—the poison that
- शल्मलौ (*śalmalau*)—in the tree called Śalmali
- भवति (*bhavati*)—is produced
- यत् (*yat*)—the poison that
- नदीषु परि (*nadīṣu pari*)—is produced from the rivers also
- यत् विषं (*yat viṣaṃ*)—the poison that
- ओषधीभ्यः (*oṣadhībhyaḥ*)—from the plants also
- जायते (*jāyate*)—is produced
- तत् (*tat*)—that poison
- विश्वे देवाः (*viśve devāḥ*)—all the gods
- इतः (*itaḥ*)—from us
- निः सुवंतु (*niḥ suvaṃtu*)—let them drive away and destroy
- सरुः (*saruḥ*)—the tortuous snake
- पद्येन (*padyena*)—of the movement of my foot
- रपसा (*rapasā*)—by the sound
- मां (*māṃ*)—my presence
- मा विदत् (*mā vidat*)—let it not know

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May all the gods drive away and destroy from us the poison that is produced in the tree called Śalmali, and the poison that is produced from the rivers. May the tortuous snake not know me by the sound of my foot's movement.

##### English translation
The poison that is in the Shalmali tree, in rivers, or which is generated from plants, may the universal gods remove from hence : let not the tortuous (snake) recognize me by the sound of my footsteps.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> याः प्र॒वतो॑ नि॒वत॑ उ॒द्वत॑ उद॒न्वती॑रनुद॒काश्च॒ याः ।

### Printed p. 302

> ता अस्मभ्यं पयसा पिन्वमानाः शिवा देवीरशिपदा भवंतु स-र्वा नद्यो अशिमिदा भवंतु ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> याः । प्रवतः । निवतः । उद्वतः । उदन्वतीः । अनुदकाः । च । याः ।  
> ताः । अस्मभ्यं । पयसा । पिन्वमानाः । शिवाः । देवीः । अशिपदाः । भवंतु । सर्वाः ।  
> नद्यः । अशिमिदाः । भवंतु ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> या नद्यः प्रवतः प्रवणदेशे गच्छंत्यः । या निवतो निम्नदेशे गच्छंत्यः । या उद्वत उन्नतदेशे गच्छंत्यः । या उदन्वतीरुदन्वत्य उदकयुक्ताः । अनुदकाश्चोदकरहिताश्च या नद्यो यंति । पयसोदकेन पिन्वमाना विश्वमाप्याययंत्यो देवीर्देव्यो द्योतमानास्तास्तादृश्यो नद्योऽस्मभ्यमशिपदाः । शिपदं नाम रोगविशेषः । तदकुर्वत्यः सत्यः शिवाः कल्याण्यो भवंतु । अपि च सर्वास्ता नद्योऽशिमिदाः । शिमिर्वधकर्मा । अंहिंसाप्रदा भवंतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The rivers that go through sloping country, those that go through low country, those that go through high country, those that are full of water (उदन्वतीः (*udanvatīḥ*)) and those without water: all rivers that flow, swelling with their water and refreshing the whole world, shining as divine ones, may they be auspicious, that is, beneficent, to us, not causing शिपदं (*śipadaṃ*), which is a particular disease. Moreover, may all those rivers be अशिमिदाः (*aśimidāḥ*): शिमि (*śimi*) is a word for killing, so they are to be givers of freedom from harm.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- याः (*yāḥ*)—the rivers that
- प्रवतः (*pravataḥ*)—flow in sloping regions
- निवतः (*nivataḥ*)—the rivers that flow in low regions
- उद्वतः (*udvataḥ*)—those that flow in high regions
- उदन्वतीः (*udanvatīḥ*)—those that are full of water
- अनुदकाश्च (*anudakāśca*)—those that are without water
- याः (*yāḥ*)—the rivers that
- पयसा (*payasā*)—with water
- पिन्वमानाः (*pinvamānāḥ*)—filling all regions
- देवीः (*devīḥ*)—are shining
- ताः (*tāḥ*)—all those rivers
- अस्मभ्यं (*asmabhyaṃ*)—to us
- अशिपदाः (*aśipadāḥ*)—not causing disease
- शिवाः (*śivāḥ*)—and bringing blessing
- भवंतु (*bhavaṃtu*)—let them be
- सर्वाः नद्यः (*sarvāḥ nadyaḥ*)—all those rivers
- अशिमिदाः (*aśimidāḥ*)—free from harm
- भवंतु (*bhavaṃtu*)—let them be

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May the rivers that flow in sloping regions, those that flow in low regions, those that flow in high regions, those that are full of water, those that are without water, and those that fill all regions with water and shine, may all those rivers not cause us disease and may they be bringers of welfare. May all those rivers be free from harm to us.

### Printed p. 303

##### English translation
May the divine rivers, whether flowing down declivities, in hollow places or upwards, whether filled with water, or dry, nourishing all with their water be auspicious to us communicating not disease : may all the rivers be unproductive of harm.

## Sūkta 51 (Maṇḍala 7)
**Introduction**

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this sūkta and the next, the deities called Ādityas are praised. "Ādityas" means the sons of Aditi. But it is necessary here to examine who these deities are, what their nature is, and the like. First we shall explain the derivation of the word āditya and afterwards take up the matter of the deities called Ādityas. The meaning "Āditya, the sun, son of Aditi" is well known. Yet on the question how the sun came by this name, Yāska has given the explanation as follows:
आदित्यः कस्मादादत्ते रसानादत्ते भासं ज्योतिषामादीप्तो भासेति वादितेः पुत्र इति वा ॥ (*ādityaḥ kasmādādatte rasānādatte bhāsaṃ jyotiṣāmādīpto bhāseti vāditeḥ putra iti vā ‖*) (Ni. 2-13)
Who is Āditya? —
(1) आदत्त इति आदित्यः (*ādatta iti ādityaḥ*). The word means one who receives, seizes or draws to himself. What does he seize? रसान् (*rasān*)—he draws the waters to himself by his rays. The water so drawn produces rain in the form of clouds. Therefore, because he draws water from the sea and other reservoirs by his rays, the sun is named Āditya.
(2) Or आदत्ते भासं ज्योतिषां (*ādatte bhāsaṃ jyotiṣāṃ*). When the sun rises, the sun's brightness dims the brightness of all other things (the moon and the rest) and draws that brightness to itself; hence the name Āditya.
(3) Or आदीप्तो भासेति वा (*ādīpto bhāseti vā*). Because he shines, covering all places or all around (by his rays), he is named Āditya.
(4) Or अदितेः पुत्र इति वा (*aditeḥ putra iti vā*). Because he is the son of the divine mother Aditi, the sun is named Āditya: this is the main sense of the explanation.

### Printed p. 304
Since the word āditya is a synonym of the sun, any of these meanings may be given. But since in the sūktas at hand the plural of the word āditya is used (आदित्यानां (*ādityānāṃ*) and the like), here the word applies not only to the sun but also to other deities. The author of the Nirukta himself says on this point:
एवमन्यासामपि देवतानामादित्यप्रवादाः स्तुतयो भवंति । तद्यथैतन्मित्रस्य वरुणस्यार्यम्णो दक्षस्य भगस्यांशस्येति ॥ (*evamanyāsāmapi devatānāmādityapravādāḥ stutayo bhavaṃti | tadyathaitanmitrasya varuṇasyāryamṇo dakṣasya bhagasyāṃśasyeti ‖*) (Ni. 2-13)
Praises addressed to the Ādityas apply also to the deities Mitra, Varuṇa, Aryamā, Dakṣa, Bhaga and Aṃśa. That is, these deities are called Ādityas (because they are sons of Aditi). Having said this, he gives as an example:
इमा गिर आदित्येभ्यो घृतस्नूः सनाद्राजभ्यो जुह्वा जुहोमि । शृणोतु मित्रो अर्यमा भगो नस्तुविजातो वरुणो दक्षो अंशः ॥ (*imā gira ādityebhyo ghṛtasnūḥ sanādrājabhyo juhvā juhomi | śṛṇotu mitro aryamā bhago nastuvijāto varuṇo dakṣo aṃśaḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-27-1)
This Ṛk is given. In this Ṛk it is explained that Mitra, Aryamā, Bhaga, Dhātā, Varuṇa, Dakṣa and Aṃśa are the Ādityas, and that they are six in number. Besides this:
अथापि मित्रावरुणयोः । आदित्या दानुनस्पती (*athāpi mitrāvaruṇayoḥ | ādityā dānunaspatī*) (Ni. 2-13)
Mitra and Varuṇa also are reckoned as Ādityas. For example:
सम्राजा घृतासुती आदित्या दानुनस्पती । सचेते अनवह्वरं ॥ (*samrājā ghṛtāsutī ādityā dānunaspatī | sacete anavahvaraṃ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-41-6)
In this Ṛk Mitra and Varuṇa are called Ādityas. Besides this:
प्र स मित्र मर्तो अस्तु प्रयस्वान्यस्त आदित्य शिक्षति व्रतेन । न हन्यते न जीयते त्वोतो नैनमंहो अश्नोत्यंतितो न दूरात् ॥ (*pra sa mitra marto astu prayasvānyasta āditya śikṣati vratena | na hanyate na jīyate tvoto nainamaṃho aśnotyaṃtito na dūrāt ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-59-2)
In this Ṛk, where Mitra alone is praised, he too is addressed as Āditya.
अथापि वरुणस्यैषः । अथा वयमादित्य व्रते तव । व्रतमिति कर्मनाम । वृणोतीति सतः ॥ (*athāpi varuṇasyaiṣaḥ | athā vayamāditya vrate tava | vratamiti karmanāma | vṛṇotīti sataḥ ‖*) (Ni. 2-13)
Even where a single deity, Varuṇa, is praised in a mantra, just as Mitra was indicated, Varuṇa too is called Āditya in that context:
उदुत्तमं वरुण पाशमस्मदवाधमं वि मध्यमं श्रथाय । अथा वयमादित्य व्रते तवानागसो अदितये स्याम ॥ (*uduttamaṃ varuṇa pāśamasmadavādhamaṃ vi madhyamaṃ śrathāya | athā vayamāditya vrate tavānāgaso aditaye syāma ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-24-15)
In this Ṛk Varuṇa is indicated as Āditya.

### Printed p. 305
Next, it is necessary to discuss closely the question of the number of the deities called Ādityas. In the Ṛk quoted above as an example, इमा गिर आदित्येभ्यः (*imā gira ādityebhyaḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-27-1), there are the names of six deities: Mitra, Aryamā, Bhaga, Varuṇa, Dakṣa and Aṃśa, and it is said there that these are the Ādityas. But for the word तुविजातः (*tuvijātaḥ*) the author of the Nirukta gives the meaning बहुजन्मा धाता (*bahujanmā dhātā*). Thus:
तुवीति बहुनाम । सर्वविधातृत्वाद्बहुजन्मा धाता यावदधिकारं जन्मेति । (*tuvīti bahunāma | sarvavidhātṛtvādbahujanmā dhātā yāvadadhikāraṃ janmeti |*).
The word तुवि (*tuvi*) means "much, many"; जन्म (*janma*) means "authority". तुविजात (*tuvijāta*) means Dhātā, the ordainer of all, the controller of all, who has authorities of many kinds. Since the word is explained thus, in this Ṛk seven should be counted as Ādityas, Dhātā being included. And in the mantra सप्त दिशो नाना सूर्याः (*sapta diśo nānā sūryāḥ*) the number of the Ādityas is given as seven. But in this Ṛk (Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-114-3) there is no mention of names; it says only देवा आदित्या ये सप्त (*devā ādityā ye sapta*). Earlier, when the matter of Aditi was being explained, the Ṛks Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-72-8, 9 were cited, and it was explained that Aditi had eight sons, that she offered seven of them to the gods, and that the eighth, Mārtāṇḍa, she kept for herself and nourished. Here too the others are not named, apart from Mārtāṇḍa. In Atharvaveda 8-9. 21 and in Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-72-8 it is stated that Aditi has eight sons. In explaining the purport of Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-27-1, Sāyaṇa, in order to state the number of the Ādityas, cites the mantra of the Taittirīya Āraṇyaka of the Yajurveda, अष्टौ पुत्रासो अदितेः । ...... । ताननुक्रमिष्यामः । मित्रश्च वरुणश्च । धाता चार्यमा च । अग्ंशश्च भगश्च । इंद्रश्च विवस्वाग्ंश्चेत्येते (*aṣṭau putrāso aditeḥ | ...... | tānanukramiṣyāmaḥ | mitraśca varuṇaśca | dhātā cāryamā ca | agṃśaśca bhagaśca | iṃdraśca vivasvāgṃścetyete*) (Tai. Ā. 1-13-3), and there he names the eight sons of Aditi: Mitra, Varuṇa, Dhātā, Aryamā, Aṃśa, Bhaga, Indra and Vivasvān.
In places such as उदगादयमादित्यः (*udagādayamādityaḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-50-12) and उदपप्तदसौ सूर्यः (*udapaptadasau sūryaḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-191-9), the identity of Sūrya and Āditya is stated. Agni, the son of Aditi, is established by the gods in the atmosphere in the form of the sun; Agni shines on earth as terrestrial fire, in the atmosphere as lightning, and in heaven as the sun. In such descriptions the name Sūrya or Āditya applies to Agni also. On the origin of the Ādityas the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa speaks as follows:
अदितिः पुत्रकामा । साध्येभ्यो देवेभ्यो ब्रह्मौदनमपचत् । तस्या उच्छेषणमददुः । तत्प्राश्नात् । सा रेतोऽधत्त । तस्यै धाता चार्यमा चाजायेतां । सा द्वितीयमपचत् । तस्या उच्छेषणमददुः । तत्प्राश्नात् । सा रेतोऽधत्त । तस्यै मित्रश्च वरुणश्चाजायेतां । सा तृतीयमपचत् । तस्या उच्छेषणमददुः । तत्प्राश्नात् । सा रेतोऽधत्त । तस्या अग्ंशश्च भगश्चाजायेतां । सा चतुर्थमपचत् । तस्या उच्छेषणमददुः । तत्प्राश्नात् । सा रेतोऽधत्त । तस्या इंद्रश्च विवस्वाग्ंश्चाजायेतां ॥ (*aditiḥ putrakāmā | sādhyebhyo devebhyo brahmaudanamapacat | tasyā uccheṣaṇamadaduḥ | tatprāśnāt | sā reto 'dhatta | tasyai dhātā cāryamā cājāyetāṃ | sā dvitīyamapacat | tasyā uccheṣaṇamadaduḥ | tatprāśnāt | sā reto 'dhatta | tasyai mitraśca varuṇaścājāyetāṃ | sā tṛtīyamapacat | tasyā uccheṣaṇamadaduḥ | tatprāśnāt | sā reto 'dhatta | tasyā agṃśaśca bhagaścājāyetāṃ | sā caturthamapacat | tasyā uccheṣaṇamadaduḥ | tatprāśnāt | sā reto 'dhatta | tasyā iṃdraśca vivasvāgṃścājāyetāṃ ‖*)

### Printed p. 306
(Tai. Brā. 1-1-9-1, 2, 3)
Aditi, desiring children, cooked a Brahmaudana oblation for the gods and the Sādhyas and offered it to them. They ate the oblation and gave her the remainder. When she ate it, by its power two sons, Dhātā and Aryamā, were born to Aditi (as twins). When the same procedure was followed a second time, Mitra and Varuṇa were born; the third time, Aṃśa and Bhaga; the fourth time, the two sons Indra and Vivasvān. The Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa describes these as the Ādityas. On this same matter the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa has this account:
स मनसा एव वाचं मिथुनं समभवत्स द्वादशगर्भ्योऽभवत् । ते द्वादश आदित्या असृज्यंत तान् दिक्षूपादधत् ॥ (*sa manasā eva vācaṃ mithunaṃ samabhavatsa dvādaśagarbhyo 'bhavat | te dvādaśa ādityā asṛjyaṃta tān dikṣūpādadhat ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 6-1-2-8)
Prajāpati entered into a mental union with Speech. Then he conceived a germ of twelve substances. Its fruit was the coming forth of the twelve Ādityas. Then he made them guardians of the quarters and placed them there, it says. In the same way, other Vedic texts that speak of the Ādityas may be examined.
कतम आदित्या इति । द्वादश वै मासाः संवत्सरस्यैत आदित्या एते हीदं सर्वमाददाना यंति ते यदिदं सर्वमाददाना यंति तस्मादादित्या इति ॥ (*katama ādityā iti | dvādaśa vai māsāḥ saṃvatsarasyaita ādityā ete hīdaṃ sarvamādadānā yaṃti te yadidaṃ sarvamādadānā yaṃti tasmādādityā iti ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 11-6-3-8)
How many Ādityas are there? The Ādityas are twelve. There are twelve months in a year; these are the Ādityas. Because those months go on taking hold of everything, they are called Ādityas, it says. The story concerning these same Ādityas is told in the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa as follows:
आदित्याश्चांगिरसश्च सुवर्गे लोके स्पर्धंत । तेंगिरस आदित्येभ्यः । अमुमादित्यमश्वग्ं श्वेतं भूतं पक्षिणामनयत् । तेऽब्रुवन् । यन्नो वेष्ट्य । सवर्यो भूदिति । तस्मादश्वग्ं सवर्येत्याह्वयंति । तस्माद्यज्ञे वरो दीयते । यत्प्रजापतिरालब्धोऽश्वोऽभवत् । तस्मादश्वो नाम । यच्छ्वेतोऽरुरासीत् । तस्मादर्वा नाम । यत्सद्यो वाजन्समजयत् । तस्माद्वाजी नाम । यदसुराणां लोकानादत्त । तस्मादादित्यो नाम ॥ (*ādityāścāṃgirasaśca suvarge loke spardhaṃta | teṃgirasa ādityebhyaḥ | amumādityamaśvagṃ śvetaṃ bhūtaṃ pakṣiṇāmanayat | te 'bruvan | yanno veṣṭya | savaryo bhūditi | tasmādaśvagṃ savaryetyāhvayaṃti | tasmādyajñe varo dīyate | yatprajāpatirālabdho 'śvo 'bhavat | tasmādaśvo nāma | yacchveto 'rurāsīt | tasmādarvā nāma | yatsadyo vājansamajayat | tasmādvājī nāma | yadasurāṇāṃ lokānādatta | tasmādādityo nāma ‖*) (Tai. Brā. 3-9-21-1, 2)
Formerly, in heaven, the Ādityas and the Aṅgirases competed with each other. The Aṅgirases offered to the Ādityas, as the sacrificial fee, the Āditya who had the form of a white horse. Then the Ādityas said to the Aṅgirases: the one that you have brought

### Printed p. 307
The Brāhmaṇa text goes on: "You have brought this Āditya, who is of the most excellent form," they said. Because the Ādityas said of the horse-formed Āditya "स वर्यः (*sa varyaḥ*)" (he is excellent), the horse too came to be called "सवर्यः (*savaryaḥ*)" in common usage. Therefore a boon is given in the sacrifice. The Prajāpati who was offered in the sacrifice is renowned as "अश्व (*aśva*)" because he pervades everything. He is "arva" because he underwent the suffering of the eye. He is "वाजी (*vājī*)" because he brought all riches under his control. He is "आदत्त (*ādatta*)" (Āditya) because he seized from the Asuras all their worlds. In this way the etymology of the word Āditya is given. The same etymology is given in the Taittirīya Āraṇyaka:
योऽसौ तपन्नुदेति । स सर्वेषां भूतानां प्राणानादायोदेति । इत्यादि (*yo 'sau tapannudeti | sa sarveṣāṃ bhūtānāṃ prāṇānādāyodeti | ityādi*) (Tai. Ā. 1-14-1).
The sun who rises seizes (ā + dā) the vital breaths of all beings and rises. For that very reason he is called Āditya. Thus two accounts are described: that the Ādityas are so called because they are sons of Aditi, or because they are marked by the act of taking (ādāna).
If the purport of the word Āditya were the description of one's own quality, it would apply only to the sun. If it shows being a son of Aditi, it applies also to Mitra, Varuṇa and the others. If the second meaning is intended, then it is not clearly stated how many sons of Aditi there are. Numbers such as six, seven, eight and twelve are stated. Some Vedic passages supporting these numbers were quoted above. In the Purāṇas and similar works the twelve Ādityas are named: Mitra, Ravi, Sūrya, Bhānu, Khaga, Pūṣan, Hiraṇyagarbha, Marīci, Āditya, Savitṛ, Arka, Bhāskara.

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> आदित्यानामिति तृचमष्टादशं सूक्तं वसिष्ठस्यार्षं त्रैष्टुभमादित्यदेवताकं । अनुक्रम्यते च । आदित्यानां तृचमादित्यं त्विति । गतः सूक्तविनियोगः । आदित्यदेवताके पशवादित्यानामवसेति वपाया अनुवाक्या । सूत्रितं च । आदित्यानामवसानूतनेनेमा गिर आदित्येभ्यः । आ. ३-८ । इति । आदित्यग्रहस्यैवानुवाक्या । सूत्रितं च । आदित्यानामवसा नूतनेन होता यक्षदादित्यान् । आ. ४-१७ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta beginning आदित्यानां (*ādityānāṃ*) is the eighteenth, of three Ṛks, of Vasiṣṭha as seer, in the Triṣṭubh metre, with the Ādityas as deity. The Anukramaṇī says: आदित्यानां तृचमादित्यं तु (*ādityānāṃ tṛcamādityaṃ tu*). The use of the sūkta has already been given. In the animal sacrifice for the Ādityas, आदित्यानामवसा (*ādityānāmavasā*) is the anuvākyā for the vapā offering. It is stated in the sūtra: आदित्यानामवसानूतनेनेमा गिर आदित्येभ्यः (*ādityānāmavasānūtanenemā gira ādityebhyaḥ*) (Ā. 3-8). It is also the anuvākyā of the Āditya graha, as the sūtra states: आदित्यानामवसा नूतनेन होता यक्षदादित्यान् (*ādityānāmavasā nūtanena hotā yakṣadādityān*) (Ā. 4-17).

Anuvāda: This sūkta beginning "आदित्यानां (*ādityānāṃ*)" is the eighteenth sūkta in the third anuvāka, with three Ṛks. Vasiṣṭha is the seer, the Ādityas the deity, Triṣṭup the metre. The Anukramaṇī says "आदित्यानां तृचमादित्यं तु (*ādityānāṃ tṛcamādityaṃ tu*)". The use of the sūkta was stated earlier. In the animal sacrifice connected with the Ādityas, the first Ṛk of this sūkta, "Ādityānām avasā", is to be recited as the anuvākyā mantra at the vapā offering, as is explained by the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra, "Ādityānām avasā nūtanene mā gira ādityebhyaḥ"

### Printed p. 308
(Ā. 3-8). In the Āditya-graha offering too this same Ṛk is to be recited as the anuvākyā mantra, as is explained by the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra, "आदित्यानामवसा नूतनेन होता यक्षदादित्यान् (*ādityānāmavasā nūtanena hotā yakṣadādityān*)" (Ā. 5-14).

Maṇḍala 7; Anuvāka 3; Sūkta 51. Aṣṭaka 5; Adhyāya 4; Varga 14. Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 3. Seer: Vasiṣṭha. Deity: the Ādityas. Metre: Triṣṭup.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ॒दि॒त्याना॒मव॑सा॒ नूत॑नेन सक्षी॒महि॒ शर्म॑णा॒ शंत॑मेन ।  
> अ॒ना॒गा॒स्त्वे अ॑दिति॒त्वे तु॒रास॑ इ॒मं य॒ज्ञं द॑धतु॒ श्रोष॑माणाः ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आदित्यानां । अवसा । नूतनेन । सक्षीमहि । शर्मणा । शंतमेन ।  
> अनागाःत्वे । अदितित्वे । तुरासः । इमं । यज्ञं । दधतु । श्रोषमाणाः ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> आदित्यानामदितेः पुत्राणामेतत्संज्ञकानां देवानामवसा रक्षणेन । तद्धेतुभूतेनेत्यर्थः । नूतनेनाद्यतनेन शंतमेन । शं सुखं । अतिशयेन तत्करणेन शर्मणा । शर्मेति गृहनामैतत् । गृहेण सक्षीमहि । वयं संगच्छेमहि । तुरासस्त्वरिता आदित्याः श्रोषमाणा अस्मदीयानि स्तोत्राणि शृण्वंतो यज्ञं यष्टारमिमं जनमनागास्त्वेनापराधत्वे चादितित्वेदीनत्वे च दधतु । स्थापयंतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"Of the Ādityas": of the sons of Aditi, the gods so named, by protection (avas), that is, by what is caused by it. By the new, that is, the present, and most comfortable (śaṃ is happiness, the maker of it in the highest degree) śarman; śarma is a name for house. With the house we would be united. The swift Ādityas, hearing our praises, would place this sacrificer in sinlessness and in being without poverty (aditi-tva, freedom from want).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- आदित्यानां (*ādityānāṃ*)—of the Ādityas
- अवसा (*avasā*)—the result of protection
- नूतनेन (*nūtanena*)—new
- शंतमेन (*śaṃtamena*)—most productive of happiness
- शर्मणा (*śarmaṇā*)—with the house
- सक्षीमहि (*sakṣīmahi*)—may we be joined
- तुरासः (*turāsaḥ*)—swift-moving
- श्रोषमाणाः (*śroṣamāṇāḥ*)—the Ādityas who listen to our praises
- यज्ञं (*yajñaṃ*)—the sacrificer
- इमं (*imaṃ*)—this patron
- अनागास्त्वे (*anāgāstve*)—in a state free from sin
- अदितित्वे (*adititve*)—in a state free from poverty
- दधतु (*dadhatu*)—may they establish

### Printed p. 309

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May we be united with the house that is the result of the Ādityas' protection, new and most productive of happiness. May the swift Ādityas, who hear our praises, establish this patron, the sacrificer, in a state free from sin and free from poverty.

##### English translation
May we, through the protection of the Adityas, be in the enjoyment of a new and comfortable dwelling : may the swift-moving Adityas, listening to our praises, preserve this their worshipper in sinlessness and independence.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ॒दि॒त्यासो॒ अदि॑तिर्मादयंतां मि॒त्रो अ॑र्य॒मा वरु॑णो॒ रजि॑ष्ठाः ।  
> अ॒स्माकं॑ संतु॒ भुव॑नस्य गो॒पाः पिबं॑तु॒ सोम॒मव॑से नो अ॒द्य ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आदित्यासः । अदितिः । मादयंतां । मित्रः । अर्यमा । वरुणः । रजिष्ठाः ।  
> अस्माकं । संतु । भुवनस्य । गोपाः । पिबंतु । सोमं । अवसे । नः । अद्य ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> आदित्यासो अदितिरित्यादित्यग्रहस्य याज्या । सूत्रितं च । आदित्यासो अदितिर्मादयंतामिति नैतं ग्रहमीक्षेते हूयमानं । आ. ५-१२ । इति ॥
> आदित्यास आदित्या देवा अदितिस्तेषां माता च । यद्वा । अदितिरिति देवविशेषणं । अदितिरदीनो देवः । रजिष्ठा अतिशयेनर्जवो मित्रोऽर्यमा वरुणश्चैतत्संज्ञका मादयंतां । तृप्ताः संतु । भुवनस्य सर्वस्य जगतो गोपा रक्षका एवंभूता देवा अस्माकं संतु । अस्माकमेव रक्षका भवंत्वित्यर्थः । अद्यास्मिन्दिने नोऽस्माकमवसे रक्षणाय सोममस्माभिरभिषुतं पिबंतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

आदित्यासो अदितिः (*ādityāso aditiḥ*) is the yājyā of the Āditya graha. The sūtra states: आदित्यासो अदितिर्मादयंतामिति नैतं ग्रहमीक्षेते हूयमानं (*ādityāso aditirmādayaṃtāmiti naitaṃ grahamīkṣete hūyamānaṃ*) (Ā. 5-12).
Ādityāsaḥ are the Āditya gods, and Aditi is their mother. Or else: "Aditi" is an epithet of a god, the god who is not poor (adīna). Mitra, Aryaman and Varuṇa, those so named, the most upright (rajiṣṭha), are to be exhilarated, that is, be satisfied. Such gods, the guardians of the whole world, are to be ours; the meaning is: let them be our protectors only. Today, on this day, for our protection, let them drink the Soma pressed by us.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- आदित्यासः (*ādityāsaḥ*)—the Ādityas
- अदितिः (*aditiḥ*)—the goddess Aditi
- रजिष्ठाः (*rajiṣṭhāḥ*)—who follow the straightest path
- मित्रः (*mitraḥ*)—Mitra
- अर्यमा (*aryamā*)—Aryaman
- वरुणः (*varuṇaḥ*)—the god Varuṇa
- मादयंतां (*mādayaṃtāṃ*)—may they find delight
- भुवनस्य (*bhuvanasya*)—of the whole world
- गोपाः (*gopāḥ*)—these gods who are protectors

### Printed p. 310
- अस्माकं (*asmākaṃ*)—as belonging to us
- संतु (*saṃtu*)—may they be
- अद्य (*adya*)—on this day of the sacrifice
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- अवसे (*avase*)—for protection
- सोमं (*somaṃ*)—the Soma
- पिबंतु (*pibaṃtu*)—may they drink

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May the Ādityas, Aditi, and Mitra, Aryaman and Varuṇa, who travel most straight, find delight. May these gods, protectors of the whole world, be ours. On this day of the sacrifice, for our protection, may they drink the Soma.

##### English translation
May the Adityas and Aditi, and the upright Mitra, Aryaman, and Varuna, be exhilarated (by the libation) : may the guardians of the world be ours also : may they drink the Soma to-day for our preservation.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

That this Ṛk, आदित्यासो अदितिः (*ādityāso aditiḥ*), is to be recited as the yājyā mantra in the Āditya-graha offering is explained by the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra: आदित्यासो अदितिर्मादयंतामिति नैतं ग्रहमीक्षेते हूयमानं (*ādityāso aditirmādayaṃtāmiti naitaṃ grahamīkṣete hūyamānaṃ*) (Ā. 5-12).

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ॒दि॒त्या विश्वे॑ म॒रुत॑श्च॒ विश्वे॑ दे॒वाश्च॒ विश्व॑ ऋ॒भव॑श्च॒ विश्वे॑ ।  
> इंद्रो॑ अ॒ग्निर॒श्विना॑ तुष्टुवा॒ना यू॒यं पा॑त स्व॒स्तिभिः॒ सदा॑ नः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आदित्याः । विश्वे । मरुतः । च । विश्वे । देवाः । च । विश्वे । ऋभवः । च । विश्वे ।  
> इंद्रः । अग्निः । अश्विना । तुस्तुवानाः । यूयं । पात । स्वस्तिभिः । सदा । नः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> आदित्या अदितेः पुत्रा विश्वे सर्वे द्वादशसंख्याका अर्काः विश्वे मरुतश्च सर्व एकोनपंचाशत्संख्योपेताश्च विश्वे देवाश्च विश्व ऋभवश्चेंद्रोऽग्निरश्विनाश्विनावेतत्संज्ञका एवंभूता ये देवास्तुष्टुवाना अस्माभिः स्तुता बभूवुः सर्वे ते देवा यूयं सदा सर्वदा नोऽस्मान् स्वस्तिभिः कल्याणैः पात । रक्षत ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The Ādityas, sons of Aditi, all, the twelve suns; all the Maruts, those of forty-nine in number; all the gods; all the Ribhus; Indra, Agni, the Aśvins: whatever gods of this kind have been praised by us, all of you, always, protect us with blessings (svasti, welfare).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- विश्वे आदित्याः (*viśve ādityāḥ*)—all the twelve Ādityas
- विश्वे मरुतः (*viśve marutaḥ*)—all the Maruts
- विश्वे देवाश्च (*viśve devāśca*)—the Viśvedevas
- विश्वे ऋभवश्च (*viśve ṛbhavaśca*)—all the Ṛbhus (the three)
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra

### Printed p. 311
- अग्निः (*agniḥ*)—Agni
- अश्विना (*aśvinā*)—the Aśvins
- तुष्टुवानाः (*tuṣṭuvānāḥ*)—have been praised by us (O all you gods of such renown)
- यूयं (*yūyaṃ*)—you
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- सदा (*sadā*)—always
- स्वस्तिभिः (*svastibhiḥ*)—with blessings
- पात (*pāta*)—protect

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

All the Ādityas, all the Maruts, the Viśvedevas, all the Ṛbhus, Indra, Agni and the Aśvins have been praised by us. O gods of such renown, protect us always with blessings.

##### English translation
All the Adityas, all the Maruts, all the gods, all the Ribhus, Indra, Agni, and the Ashwins, (have been) glorified (by us) : do you all ever cherish us with blessings.

## Sūkta 52 (Maṇḍala 7)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> आदित्यासो अदितय इति तृचात्मकमेकोनविंशं सूक्तं वसिष्ठस्यार्षं त्रैष्टुभमादित्यदेवताकं । आदित्यास इत्यनुक्रमणिका ॥ गतो विनियोगः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta beginning आदित्यासो अदितयः (*ādityāso aditayaḥ*) is the nineteenth, consisting of three Ṛks, of Vasiṣṭha as seer, in Triṣṭubh metre, with the Ādityas as deity. The Anukramaṇī says आदित्यास (*ādityāsa*). The use has already been given.

Anuvāda: This sūkta beginning "आदित्यासो अदितयः (*ādityāso aditayaḥ*)" is the nineteenth sūkta in the third anuvāka, with three Ṛks. Vasiṣṭha is the seer, the Ādityas the deity, Triṣṭup the metre. The Anukramaṇī says "आदित्यासः (*ādityāsaḥ*)". The use was stated earlier.

Maṇḍala 7; Anuvāka 3; Sūkta 52. Aṣṭaka 5; Adhyāya 4; Varga 15. Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 3. Seer: Vasiṣṭha. Deity: the Ādityas. Metre: Triṣṭup.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ॒दि॒त्यासो॒ अदि॑तयः स्याम॒ पूर्दे॑व॒त्रा व॑सवो मर्त्य॒त्रा ।  
> सने॑म मित्रावरुणा॒ सनं॑तो॒ भवे॑म द्यावापृथिवी॒ भवं॑तः ॥

### Printed p. 312

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आदित्यासः । अदितयः । स्याम । पूः । देवत्रा । वसवः । मर्त्यत्रा ।  
> सनेम । मित्रावरुणा । सनंतः । भवेम । द्यावापृथिवी इति । भवंतः ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे आदित्यासो आदित्या देवाः ॥ व्यत्ययेनासाद्युदात्तत्वाभावः ॥ यद्वा ॥ आदित्यानामिम आदित्याः ॥ तस्येदमित्यर्थे प्राग्दीव्यतीयो ण्यप्रत्ययः ॥ आदित्यानां शेषभूता वयमदितयोऽखंडनीयाः स्याम । भवेम । देवत्रा देवेषु वसवो वासका देवाः युष्मदीयं पूः पालनं मर्त्यत्रा मनुष्येष्वस्मासु भवतु । हे मित्रावरुणा मित्रावरुणौ सनंतो युवां संभजंतो वयं सनेम । युवाभ्यां दत्तं धनं संभजेमहि । हे द्यावापृथिवी द्यावापृथिव्यौ युवयोः प्रसादाद्वयं भवंतो भवेम । भूतिमंतः स्याम ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Ādityas, gods; the loss of the ādyudātta accent in आदित्यासो (*ādityāso*) is by vyatyaya. Or else: those belonging to the Ādityas are Ādityas (a suffix ṇya in the sense "this is his"). We, being the remainder (śeṣa) of the Ādityas, would be aditi, unbroken (akhaṇḍanīya); would be. Among the gods, O Vasus, gods who dwell (vāsaka), let your protection (pūḥ) be with us, mortals. O Mitra and Varuṇa, we, partaking of you (sanantaḥ), would partake: we would share the wealth given by you. O heaven and earth, by your favour we, being, would be: we would be possessed of prosperity.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- आदित्यासः (*ādityāsaḥ*)—O Ādityas
- अदितयः (*aditayaḥ*)—with a life without any deficiency (we)
- स्याम (*syāma*)—may we be
- देवत्रा (*devatrā*)—among all the gods
- वसवः (*vasavaḥ*)—O revered Vasus
- पूः (*pūḥ*)—your act of protection
- मर्त्यत्रा (*martyatrā*)—may it relate to us, human beings
- मित्रावरुणा (*mitrāvaruṇā*)—O Mitra and Varuṇa
- सनंतः (*sanaṃtaḥ*)—we who worship you
- सनेम (*sanema*)—may we enjoy the wealth given by you
- द्यावापृथिवी (*dyāvāpṛthivī*)—O heaven and earth
- भवंतः (*bhavaṃtaḥ*)—we who are worthy of your favour
- भवेम (*bhavema*)—may we be possessed of prosperity

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Ādityas, grant that we may have a life without any deficiency. O Vasus, revered among all the gods, may your act of protection relate to us human beings. O Mitra and Varuṇa, may we who worship you enjoy the wealth given by you. O heaven and earth, grant that we, worthy of your favour, may be possessed of prosperity.

##### English translation
Adityas, may we be independent : Vasus, (dwelling) among the gods, may your protection (extend) to mortals : offering (oblations to you), Mitra and Varuna, may we partake (of your bounty) : may we, heaven and earth, exist (through your favour).

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> मि॒त्रस्तन्नो॒ वरु॑णो मामहंत॒ शर्म॑ तो॒काय॒ तन॑याय गो॒पाः ।

### Printed p. 313

> मा वो भुजेमान्यजातमेनो मा तत्कर्म वसवो यच्चयध्वे ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> मित्रः । तत् । नः । वरुणः । ममहंत । शर्म । तोकाय । तनयाय । गोपाः ।  
> मा । वः । भुजेम । अन्यजातं । एनः । मा । तत् । कर्म । वसवः । यत् ।  
> चयध्वे ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> मित्रो वरुणोऽहर्निशाभिमानिनौ देवावेतदाद्याः सर्व आदित्या नोऽस्मभ्यं तत्प्रसिद्धं शर्म सुखं ममहंत । महतिर्दानकर्मा । ददतु । गोपा विश्वस्य रक्षकास्ते देवास्तोकायास्मदीयाय पुत्राय तनयाय तत्पुत्राय च शर्म प्रयच्छंतु । अथ प्रत्यक्षस्तुतिः । हे देवाः वो युष्मदीया वयमन्यजातमन्येनोत्पादितमेनः पापं मा भुजेम । मा भुक्तवंतः स्याम । हे वसवो वासका देवाः यद्येन युष्मदप्रियेण कर्मणा चयध्वे यूयमस्मान्नाशयत तत्तादृशं कर्म वयं मा कर्म । मा कार्ष्म ॥ लुङि करोतेरुत्तमस्य बहुवचनं । मंत्रे घसह्वरेत्यादिना च्लेर्लोपः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Mitra and Varuna, the deities presiding over day and night, and all the Ādityas beginning with them, are to give us that well-known comfort (ममहंत (*mamahaṃta*); the root महति (*mahati*) has the sense of giving). Being the guardians of the whole world, those gods are to bestow comfort on our son and on his son. Then comes a direct address: O gods, may we, who are yours, not enjoy the sin produced by another. O Vasus, the sheltering gods, may we not do that deed, disagreeable to you, by which you destroy us.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मित्रः (*mitraḥ*)—Mitra
- वरुणः (*varuṇaḥ*)—and Varuna
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- तत् (*tat*)—the well-known
- शर्म (*śarma*)—comfort
- ममहंत (*mamahaṃta*)—may they give
- गोपाः (*gopāḥ*)—the gods beginning with the Ādityas, protectors of the world
- तोकाय (*tokāya*)—to our sons
- तनयाय (*tanayāya*)—and to their sons, may they bring about that comfort
- वः (*vaḥ*)—(O gods) we who belong to you
- अन्यजातं (*anyajātaṃ*)—done by another
- एनः (*enaḥ*)—the fruit of the sin
- मा भुजेम (*mā bhujema*)—may we not come to enjoy
- वसवः (*vasavaḥ*)—O gods called Vasus
- यत् (*yat*)—by what sinful action
- चयध्वे (*cayadhve*)—you may destroy us
- तत् (*tat*)—such a sinful deed
- मा कर्म (*mā karma*)—may we not do

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May Mitra and Varuna give us the world-famous comfort. May the gods beginning with the Ādityas, protectors of the world, give that comfort to our sons and their sons. O gods, may we, who belong to you, not have to enjoy the fruit of a sin committed by another. O gods called Vasus, may we not commit any sinful deed by which you might destroy us.

### Printed p. 314

##### English translation
May Mitra and Varuna bestow upon us felicity : (may they) the guardians (of all men, bestow felicity) upon our sons and grandsons : let us not suffer (gods), for offences committed by another : let us not, Vasus, do any act by which you may be offended.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> तु॒र॒ण्यवोऽंगि॑रसो नक्षंत॒ रत्नं॑ दे॒वस्य॑ सवि॒तुरि॑या॒नाः ।  
> पि॒ता च॒ तन्नो॑ म॒हान्यज॑त्रो॒ विश्वे॑ दे॒वाः सम॑नसो जुषंत ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> तुरण्यवः । अंगिरसः । नक्षंत । रत्नं । देवस्य । सवितुः । इयानाः ।  
> पिता । च । तत् । नः । महान् । यजत्रः । विश्वे । देवाः । समनसः । जुषंत ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> तुरण्यवो यज्ञादिकर्मसु त्वरिता अंगिरस एतन्नामका ऋषय इयानाः सवितारं याचमानाः संतः सवितुः प्रेरकस्य देवस्य संबंधि रत्नं रमणीयं यद्धनं नक्षंत आश्नुवंते । उत्तरार्धगततच्छब्दापेक्षया यच्छब्दोऽत्राध्याह्रियते । यजत्रो यजनशीलो महान् प्रभूतः पिता च वसिष्ठस्य पितृभूतो वरुणश्च । यद्वा । सर्वेषां पिता प्रजापतिः । विश्वे सर्वे देवाः समनसः समानमनस्कास्तत्तादृशं रत्नं नोऽस्माञ्जुषंते । सेवयंतु । यद्वा । नोऽस्मभ्यं ददतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The Aṅgirases, a class of seers of that name who are quick in sacrificial acts, beg Savitṛ, the impelling god, and obtain the delightful wealth that belongs to him. The word यत् (*yat*) is supplied here, in view of the तत् (*tat*) of the second half. The adorable, great, abundant "father" is either Varuṇa, father of Vasiṣṭha, or, alternatively, Prajāpati, father of all. All the gods, of one mind, are to favour us with that wealth; or alternatively, they are to give it to us.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- तुरण्यवः (*turaṇyavaḥ*)—devoted to sacrificial work
- अंगिरसः (*aṃgirasaḥ*)—the Aṅgirases
- इयानाः (*iyānāḥ*)—begging Savitṛ
- देवस्य (*devasya*)—divine
- सवितुः (*savituḥ*)—from the god Savitṛ
- रत्नं (*ratnaṃ*)—attractive wealth
- नक्षंत (*nakṣaṃta*)—obtain
- यजत्रः (*yajatraḥ*)—the venerable
- महान् (*mahān*)—of great nature
- पिता च (*pitā ca*)—and Prajāpati, father of the world (or Varuṇa, father of Vasiṣṭha)
- विश्वे देवाः (*viśve devāḥ*)—all the gods
- समनसः (*samanasaḥ*)—being of one mind
- तत् (*tat*)—by giving that well-known wealth
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- जुषंत (*juṣaṃta*)—may they please

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The Aṅgirases, devoted to sacrificial work, beg Savitṛ and obtain attractive wealth from the divine Savitṛ. May the venerable, great Prajāpati, father of the world, and all the gods, of one mind, please us by giving the well-known wealth.

### Printed p. 315

##### English translation
The Angirasas, prompt (in worship), soliciting him, obtain precious (wealth) from Savitri : may the mighty adorable parent, and the universal god, alike favourably minded, approve (of the donation).

## Sūkta 53 (Maṇḍala 7)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> प्र द्यावा यज्ञैरिति तृचात्मकं विंशं सूक्तं वसिष्ठस्यार्षं त्रैष्टुभं द्यावापृथिव्यं । अनुक्रम्यते च । प्र द्यावा द्यावापृथिव्यमिति ॥ चतुर्थेऽहनि वैश्वदेवशस्त्रे इदं द्यावापृथिव्यनिविद्धानं । सूत्रितं च । आ देवो यातु प्र द्यावेति वासिष्ठं । आ. ८-८ । इति ॥ द्यावापृथिव्ये पशौ वपापुरोडाशयोः प्र द्यावेति द्वे ऋचौ याज्ये । सूत्रितं च । प्र द्यावा यज्ञैः पृथिवी नमोभिरिति द्वे । आ. ३-८ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta प्र द्यावा यज्ञैः (*pra dyāvā yajñaiḥ*) consists of three ṛks; it is the twentieth sūkta; seer Vasiṣṭha, metre Triṣṭubh, deity Dyāvāpṛthivī; the Anukramaṇī says प्र द्यावा द्यावापृथिव्यं (*pra dyāvā dyāvāpṛthivyaṃ*). On the fourth day, in the Vaiśvadeva śastra, it serves as the Dyāvāpṛthivī nivid-dhāna; the sūtra is आ देवो यातु प्र द्यावेति वासिष्ठं (*ā devo yātu pra dyāveti vāsiṣṭhaṃ*) (Ā. 8-8). In the animal offering to Dyāvāpṛthivī, the two ṛks प्र द्यावा (*pra dyāvā*) are the yājyās of the vapā and the puroḍāśa; the sūtra is प्र द्यावा यज्ञैः पृथिवी नमोभिरिति द्वे (*pra dyāvā yajñaiḥ pṛthivī namobhiriti dve*) (Ā. 3-8).

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta beginning प्र द्यावा यज्ञैः (*pra dyāvā yajñaiḥ*) is the twentieth sūkta in the third anuvāka, with three ṛks. Its seer is Vasiṣṭha; the deity is Dyāvāpṛthivī; the metre is Triṣṭubh. The Anukramaṇī says: प्र द्यावा द्यावापृथिव्यं (*pra dyāvā dyāvāpṛthivyaṃ*). On the fourth day of the sacrifice, when the Vaiśvadeva śastra mantras are recited, this sūkta is to be recited as a nivid-dhāna sūkta addressed to Dyāvāpṛthivī, as explained by the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra आ देवो यातु प्र द्यावेति वासिष्ठं (*ā devo yātu pra dyāveti vāsiṣṭhaṃ*) (Ā. 8-8). In the animal sacrifice offered to Dyāvāpṛthivī, the first two ṛks of this sūkta are to be recited as yājyā mantras in the vapā and puroḍāśa offerings respectively, as explained by the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra प्र द्यावा यज्ञैः पृथिवी नमोभिरिति द्वे (*pra dyāvā yajñaiḥ pṛthivī namobhiriti dve*) (Ā. 3-8).

##### Particulars

- Sūkta 53
- Maṇḍala 7; Anuvāka 3; Sūkta 53
- Aṣṭaka 5; Adhyāya 4; Varga 17
- Number of ṛks in the sūkta: 3
- Ṛṣi: Vasiṣṭha
- Devatā: Dyāvāpṛthivī (dual)
- Chandas: Triṣṭubh

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्र द्यावा॑ य॒ज्ञैः पृ॑थि॒वी नमो॑भिः स॒ुबाध॑ ईळे बृह॒ती यज॑त्रे ।

### Printed p. 316

> ते चिद्धि पूर्वे कवयो गृणंतः पुरो मही दधिरे देवपुत्रे ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्र । द्यावा । यज्ञैः । पृथिवी इति । नमःऽभिः । सऽबाधः । ईळे । बृहती इति । यजत्रे इति ।  
> ते इति । चित् । हि । पूर्वे । कवयः । गृणंतः । पुरः । मही इति । दधिरे । देवपुत्रे इति देवपुत्रे ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यजत्रे यजनीये बृहती बृहत्यौ महत्यौ द्यावापृथिवी द्यावापृथिव्यौ यज्ञैर्गीर्भिर्नमोभिः स्तोत्रैश्चाहं स्तोता सबाधो बाधासहितः । ऋत्विजां संबाधयुक्त इत्यर्थः । प्रेळे । प्रकर्षेण स्तौमि । मही महत्यौ देवपुत्रे । देवाः पुत्राः ययोस्ते । ते चिद्धि तादृश्यौ खल्वपि द्यावापृथिव्यौ पूर्वे पुरातनाः कवयो गृणंतः स्तुवंतः पुरो दधिरे । पुरस्तात्स्थापयामासुः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The adorable, great Heaven and Earth, whose sons are the gods, are praised by me, the praiser, with sacrifices (यज्ञैः (*yajñaiḥ*), hymns) and with praises (नमोभिः (*namobhiḥ*), laudations), accompanied by difficulty (सबाधः (*sabādhaḥ*)), that is, pressed by the priests' crowding. I praise them intensely. Those very two, Heaven and Earth, the ancient seers, praising them, placed in front.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यजत्रे (*yajatre*)—worthy of worship
- बृहती (*bṛhatī*)—of great nature
- द्यावापृथिवी (*dyāvāpṛthivī*)—Heaven and Earth
- यज्ञैः (*yajñaiḥ*)—with sacrifices
- नमोभिः (*namobhiḥ*)—with obeisance
- सबाधः (*sabādhaḥ*)—accompanied by the priests
- प्र ईळे (*pra īḷe*)—I praise and worship
- मही (*mahī*)—of great nature
- देवपुत्रे (*devaputre*)—who have the gods as sons
- ते चिद्धि (*te ciddhi*)—those very Heaven and Earth
- पूर्वे (*pūrve*)—ancient
- कवयः (*kavayaḥ*)—seers
- गृणंतः (*gṛṇaṃtaḥ*)—praising
- पुरः दधिरे (*puraḥ dadhire*)—placed in front in the sacrificial portion

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

I praise and worship, with sacrifices, with obeisance, and accompanied by priests, Heaven and Earth, who are worthy of worship and of great nature. The ancient seers praised those very Heaven and Earth, of great nature and having the gods for sons, and placed them in front in the sacrificial portion.

##### English translation
Attended by a concourse (of priests), I worship the adorable and mighty Heaven and Earth with sacrifices and praises, those two great ones

### Printed p. 317
of whom the gods are the sons, whom ancient sages glorifying have formerly detained.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्र पू॑र्व॒जे पि॒तरा॒ नव्य॑सीभिर्गी॒र्भिः कृ॑णुध्वं॒ सद॑ने ऋ॒तस्य॑ ।  
> आ नो॑ द्यावापृथिवी॒ दैव्ये॑न॒ जने॑न यातं॒ महि॑ वां॒ वरू॑थं॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्र । पूर्वजे इति पूर्वऽजे । पितरा । नव्यसीभिः । गीऽभिः । कृणुध्वं । सदने इति । ऋतस्य ।  
> आ । नः । द्यावापृथिवी इति । दैव्येन । जनेन । यातं । महि । वां । वरूथं ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> आग्रयणे द्यावापृथिव्यैककपालस्य प्र पूर्वजे इति याज्या । सूक्तं च । मही द्यौः पृथिवी च नः प्र पूर्वजे पितरा नव्यसीभिः । आ. २-९ । इति ॥
> हे अस्मदीयाः स्तोतारः यूयं नव्यसीभिर्नवतराभिर्गीर्भिः स्तुतिरूपाभिर्वाग्भिर्ऋतस्य यज्ञस्य सदने स्थानभूते पूर्वजे पूर्वं प्रजाते पितरा पितरौ विश्वस्य मातापितृभूते द्यावापृथिव्यौ प्र कृणुध्वं । पुरस्कुरुत । अथ प्रत्यक्षस्तुतिः । हे द्यावापृथिवी द्यावापृथिव्यौ युवां दैव्येन देवसंबंधिना जनेन सह नोस्मानभ्या यातं । आगच्छतं । किमर्थमायातमिति उच्यते । वां युवयोर्वरूथं मस्माभिर्वरणीयं महि महत् । यद्धनमस्तीति शेषः । तद्धनमस्मभ्यं दीयतामित्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In the Āgrayaṇa, the ṛk प्र पूर्वजे (*pra pūrvaje*) is the yājyā for the single-potsherd offering to Dyāvāpṛthivī; the sūtra is मही द्यौः पृथिवी च नः प्र पूर्वजे पितरा नव्यसीभिः (*mahī dyauḥ pṛthivī ca naḥ pra pūrvaje pitarā navyasībhiḥ*) (Ā. 2-9). Sāyaṇa addresses our praisers: with newer songs, in the form of praises, set in front Heaven and Earth, born first, parents of the whole world, who are the seat of the sacrifice (ऋतस्य (*ṛtasya*)). Then a direct address: O Heaven and Earth, come to us with the divine people. Why come? Your great (महि (*mahi*)) वरूथं (*varūthaṃ*), to be chosen by us, is wealth that exists (the verb is supplied); let that wealth be given to us.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O gods) नव्यसीभिः (*navyasībhiḥ*)—newly composed
- गीर्भिः (*gīrbhiḥ*)—with praises
- ऋतस्य (*ṛtasya*)—of the sacrifice
- सदने (*sadane*)—who are the support
- पूर्वजे (*pūrvaje*)—first born
- पितरा (*pitarā*)—Heaven and Earth, parents of the whole world
- प्र कृणुध्वं (*pra kṛṇudhvaṃ*)—honour first and worship
- द्यावापृथिवी (*dyāvāpṛthivī*)—O Heaven and Earth
- दैव्येन (*daivyena*)—related to the gods
- जनेन (*janena*)—with the host of people
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- आ यातं (*ā yātaṃ*)—come
- वां (*vāṃ*)—which belongs to you
- वरूथं (*varūthaṃ*)—desired by us
- महि (*mahi*)—abundant wealth, give it to us

### Printed p. 318

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O praisers, with newly composed praises honour first and worship Heaven and Earth, support of the sacrifice, first born, parents of the whole world. O Heaven and Earth, come to our sacrifice with the divine people, to give us abundantly the wealth that we desire and that fills you.

##### English translation
Place before (us) in the hall of sacrifice, with new songs, the ancient parents (of all beings) : come to us, Heaven and Earth, with the celestial people, for vast is your protecting (wealth).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In the Āgrayaṇeṣṭi, in the single-potsherd offering made to the deities Dyāvāpṛthivī, this ṛk प्र पूर्वजे (*pra pūrvaje*) is to be recited as the yājyā mantra, as explained by the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra मही द्यौः पृथिवी च नः प्र पूर्वजे पितरा नव्यसीभिः (*mahī dyauḥ pṛthivī ca naḥ pra pūrvaje pitarā navyasībhiḥ*) (Ā. 2-9).

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒तो हि वां॑ रत्न॒धेया॑नि॒ संति॑ पु॒रूणि॑ द्यावापृथिवी सु॒दासे॑ ।  
> अ॒स्मे ध॑त्तं॒ यदस॒दस्कृ॑धोयु यू॒यं पा॑त स्व॒स्तिभिः॒ सदा॑ नः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उतो इति । हि । वां । रत्नऽधेयानि । संति । पुरूणि । द्यावापृथिवी इति । सुदासे ।  
> अस्मे इति । धत्तं । यत् । असत् । अस्कृधोयु । यूयं । पात । स्वस्तिभिः । सदा । नः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> उतोपि च खलु हे द्यावापृथिवी द्यावापृथिव्यौ वां युवयोः सुदासे शोभनहविर्दानाय यजमानाय देयानि पुरूणि बहूनि रत्नधेयानि रमणीयानि धनानि संति । भवंति । तेषां मध्ये यद्धनमस्कृधोयु । कृधुको ह्रस्वः । अह्रस्वमनल्पमसत् भवेत् तद्धनमस्मे अस्मभ्यं धत्तं । प्रयच्छतं । हे द्यावापृथिव्यौ यूयं युवां नोस्मान् सर्वदा कल्याणैः पात । पालयत ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Heaven and Earth, there are for the generous giver of oblations many delightful treasures (रत्नधेयानि (*ratnadheyāni*)) that are yours to give. Among them, the wealth that is अस्कृधोयु (*askṛdhoyu*) (कृधुको ह्रस्वः (*kṛdhuko hrasvaḥ*), short), that is, not small, be that wealth, grant it to us. O Heaven and Earth, protect us always with blessings.

### Printed p. 319

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- द्यावापृथिवी (*dyāvāpṛthivī*)—O Heaven and Earth
- उतो हि (*uto hi*)—moreover
- वां (*vāṃ*)—in you
- सुदासे (*sudāse*)—to be given to the sacrificer who offers the oblation
- रत्नधेयानि (*ratnadheyāni*)—delightful treasures
- पुरूणि (*purūṇi*)—in great abundance
- संति (*saṃti*)—there are
- यत् (*yat*)—which portion of them
- अस्कृधोयु (*askṛdhoyu*)—in great abundance
- असत् (*asat*)—is, that
- अस्मे (*asme*)—to us
- धत्तं (*dhattaṃ*)—give
- यूयं (*yūyaṃ*)—you
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- सदा (*sadā*)—always
- स्वस्तिभिः (*svastibhiḥ*)—with blessings
- पात (*pāta*)—protect

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Heaven and Earth, the delightful treasures that are to be given to the sacrificer who offers the oblation are present in you in great abundance. Whichever portion of them is in great abundance, give that to us. O Heaven and Earth, protect us always with blessings.

##### English translation
Many, Heaven and Earth, are the treasures fit to be given to the pious donor (of the oblation) : give to us that wealth which is unlimited : and do you ever cherish us with blessings.

## Sūkta 54 (Maṇḍala 7)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> वास्तोष्पते इति तृचात्मकमेकविंशं सूक्तं वसिष्ठस्यार्षं त्रैष्टुभं वास्तोष्पत्यं । तथा चानुक्रम्यते । वास्तोष्पते वास्तोष्पत्यमिति ॥ स्मार्ते गृहनिर्माणे वास्तोष्पत इति चतसृभिः प्रत्यृचं जुहुयात् । सूत्रितं च । वास्तोष्पते प्रति जानीह्यस्मानिति चतसृभिः प्रत्यृचं हुत्वा । आ. गृ. २-९-६ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The twenty-first sūkta, beginning वास्तोष्पते (*vāstoṣpate*), consists of three Ṛks; its seer is Vasiṣṭha, its metre Triṣṭubh, its deity Vāstoṣpati. The Anukramaṇī says: वास्तोष्पते वास्तोष्पत्यमिति (*vāstoṣpate vāstoṣpatyamiti*). In the smārta rite of house-building one should offer an oblation with each Ṛk of the four beginning वास्तोष्पत इति (*vāstoṣpata iti*). The sūtra says: वास्तोष्पते प्रति जानीह्यस्मानिति चतसृभिः प्रत्यृचं हुत्वा (*vāstoṣpate prati jānīhyasmāniti catasṛbhiḥ pratyṛcaṃ hutvā*) (Ā. Gṛ. 2-9-6).**NOTE**

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta, वास्तोष्पते (*vāstoṣpate*), is the twenty-first sūkta in the third anuvāka. It has three Ṛks. Vasiṣṭha is the seer of this sūkta; Vāstoṣpati is the deity; the metre is Triṣṭubh. In the Anukramaṇī it is said वास्तोष्पते वास्तोष्पत्यं (*vāstoṣpate vāstoṣpatyaṃ*). In the house-building rite prescribed in the Smṛti and other works, the three Ṛks of this sūkta together with the first Ṛk of the next sūkta, four Ṛks in all, are to be used for an oblation, one offering with each Ṛk; this is explained by the Āśvalāyana Gṛhya Sūtra, वास्तोष्पते प्रति जानीह्यस्मानिति चतसृभिः प्रत्यृचं हुत्वा (*vāstoṣpate prati jānīhyasmāniti catasṛbhiḥ pratyṛcaṃ hutvā*) (Ā. Gṛ. 2-9-6).

### Printed p. 320
#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वास्तो॑ष्पते॒ प्रति॑ जानीह्य॒स्मान्स्वा॑वे॒शो अ॑नमी॒वो भ॑वा नः  
> यत्त्वेम॑हे॒ प्रति॒ तन्नो॑ जुषस्व॒ शं नो॑ भव द्वि॒पदे॒ शं चतु॑ष्पदे ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वास्तोः । पते । प्रति । जानीहि । अस्मान् । सुऽआवेशः । अनमीवः । भव । नः ।  
> यत् । त्वा । ईमहे । प्रति । तत् । नः । जुषस्व । शं । नः । भव । द्विऽपदे । शं । चतुःऽपदे ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे वास्तोष्पते गृहस्य पालयितर्देव त्वमस्मांस्त्वदीयान् स्तोतॄनिति प्रति जानीहि । प्रबुध्यस्व । तदनंतरं नोस्माकं स्वावेशः शोभननिवेशोनमीवोरोगकृच्च भव । किंच वयं त्वा त्वां यद्धनमीमहे याचामहे त्वमपि तद्धनं नोस्मभ्यं प्रति जुषस्व । प्रयच्छ । अपि च नोस्माकं द्विपदे पुत्रपौत्रादिजनाय शं सुखकरो भव । चतुष्पदेस्मदीयाय गवाश्वादिवर्गाय च शं सुखकरो भव ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Vāstoṣpati, protector of the house, recognise us as your own worshippers; awake. After that, be to us a good dwelling and one who causes no disease. Further, whatever wealth we ask of you, grant it to us. Be a source of happiness to our bipeds, that is, sons, grandsons and other people, and to our quadrupeds, that is, our cattle, horses and the like.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वास्तोष्पते (*vāstoṣpate*)—O Vāstoṣpati, protector of the house
- अस्मान् (*asmān*)—us
- प्रति जानीहि (*prati jānīhi*)—know as your worshippers
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- स्वावेशः (*svāveśaḥ*)—giver of an excellent dwelling
- अनमीवः (*anamīvaḥ*)—destroyer of disease
- भव (*bhava*)—be
- त्वा (*tvā*)—from you
- यत् (*yat*)—which wealth
- ईमहे (*īmahe*)—we ask
- तत् (*tat*)—that wealth
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- प्रति जुषस्व (*prati juṣasva*)—give
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- द्विपदे (*dvipade*)—to sons, grandsons and others
- शं (*śaṃ*)—as the bringer of happiness
- भव (*bhava*)—be
- चतुष्पदे (*catuṣpade*)—to cattle and others
- शं (*śaṃ*)—be the bringer of happiness

### Printed p. 321

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Vāstoṣpati, protector of the house, know us as your worshippers. Be to us a giver of an excellent dwelling and a destroyer of disease. Whatever wealth we ask of you, give that wealth to us. Be a bringer of happiness to our sons, grandsons and others, and to our cattle and the like.

##### English translation
Protector of the dwelling, recognize us : be to us an excellent abode, the non-inflicter of disease : whatever we ask of you, be pleased to grant : be the bestower of happiness on our bipeds and quadrupeds.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

For the particulars concerning the deity called Vāstoṣpati, see the Special Topics on the first Ṛk of the 55th sūkta of this same portion.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वास्तो॑ष्पते प्र॒तर॑णो न एधि गय॒स्फानो॒ गोभि॒रश्वे॑भिरिंदो ।  
> अ॒जरा॑सस्ते स॒ख्ये स्या॑म पि॒तेव॑ पु॒त्रान्प्रति॑ नो जुषस्व ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वास्तोः । पते । प्रऽतरणः । नः । एधि । गयःऽस्फानः । गोभिः । अश्वेभिः । इंदो इति ।  
> अजरासः । ते । सख्ये । स्याम । पिताऽइव । पुत्रान् । प्रति । नः । जुषस्व ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे वास्तोष्पते गृहस्य पालयितर्देव त्वं नोस्माकं प्रतरणः प्रवर्धको गयस्फानो गयस्य धनस्य स्फाययिता प्रवर्धक एधि । भव । हे इंदो सोमवदाह्लादक ते त्वया सह सख्ये सति वयं गोभिः पशुभिरश्वेभिरश्वैश्च सहिता अजरासो जरारहिताः स्याम । भवेम । विनाशरहिता इत्यर्थः । पितेव पुत्रान् यथा पिता पुत्रान्प्रकृष्टेन सेवते तथा त्वमपि नोस्मान्प्रति जुषस्व । सेवस्व ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Vāstoṣpati, protector of the house, be our promoter, the increaser of गय (*gaya*), that is, of wealth. O Indu, gladdener like Soma, when there is friendship with you, may we, together with cattle and horses, be free from old age, that is, free from destruction. As a father cherishes his sons with devotion, so do you also cherish us.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वास्तोष्पते (*vāstoṣpate*)—O Vāstoṣpati
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- प्रतरणः (*prataraṇaḥ*)—a promoter of growth
- गयस्फानः (*gayasphānaḥ*)—the increaser of our house
- एधि (*edhi*)—be
- इंदो (*iṃdo*)—O delighting god
- ते (*te*)—with you
- सख्ये (*sakhye*)—there being friendship
- (वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we)
- गोभिः (*gobhiḥ*)—with cattle
- अश्वेभिः (*aśvebhiḥ*)—and with horses, endowed
- अजरासः (*ajarāsaḥ*)—free from decay
- स्याम (*syāma*)—may we live

### Printed p. 322
- पिता (*pitā*)—a father
- पुत्रान् इव (*putrān iva*)—as he cherishes and protects his children
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- प्रति जुषस्व (*prati juṣasva*)—cherish

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Vāstoṣpati, be a promoter of growth for us and the increaser of our house. O gladdening god, with friendship with you may we live together with cattle and horses, free from decay. As a father cherishes and protects his children, so cherish us with affection.

##### English translation
Protector of the dwelling, be our preserver and the augmenter of our wealth : possessed of cattle and horses, Indra, may we, through your friendship, be exempt from decay : be favourable to us like a father to his sons.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वास्तो॑ष्पते श॒ग्मया॑ सं॒सदा॑ ते सक्षी॒महि॑ र॒ण्वया॑ गातु॒मत्या॑ ।  
> पा॒हि क्षेम॑ उ॒त योगे॒ वरं॑ नो यू॒यं पा॑त स्व॒स्तिभिः॒ सदा॑ नः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वास्तोः । पते । शग्मया । संऽसदा । ते । सक्षीमहि । रण्वया । गातुऽमत्या ।  
> पाहि । क्षेमे । उत । योगे । वरं । नः । यूयं । पात । स्वस्तिभिः । सदा । नः ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे वास्तोष्पते देव शग्मया सुखकर्या रण्वया रमणीयया गातुमत्या धनवत्या ते त्वया देयया संसदा स्थानेन सक्षीमहि । वयं संगच्छेमहि । त्वमपि क्षेमे प्राप्तस्यरक्षणे उतापि च योगेप्राप्तस्य प्रापणे वरं वरणीयं नोस्मदीयं धनं पाहि । रक्ष । हे वास्तोष्पते यूयं त्वं नोस्मान् सर्वदा कल्याणैः पात । पाहि ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O god Vāstoṣpati, may we be united with the abode given by you, which is comfort-giving (शग्मया (*śagmayā*)), delightful (रण्वया (*raṇvayā*)) and wealthy (गातुमत्या (*gātumatyā*)). In क्षेमे (*kṣeme*), the keeping of what has been obtained, and in योगे (*yoge*), the obtaining of what has not been obtained, protect our choice wealth. O Vāstoṣpati, you protect us always with blessings.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वास्तोष्पते (*vāstoṣpate*)—O Vāstoṣpati
- शग्मया (*śagmayā*)—that which produces happiness
- रण्वया (*raṇvayā*)—delightful
- गातुमत्या (*gātumatyā*)—wealthy
- ते (*te*)—and given by you
- संसदा (*saṃsadā*)—with the house
- सक्षीमहि (*sakṣīmahi*)—may we be united
- उत (*uta*)—and
- क्षेमे (*kṣeme*)—

### Printed p. 323
- by protecting us
- योगे (*yoge*)—by bringing us what we desire
- वरं (*varaṃ*)—excellent
- नः (*naḥ*)—our wealth
- पाहि (*pāhi*)—protect
- (O Vāstoṣpati and the other gods) यूयं (*yūyaṃ*)—you
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- सदा (*sadā*)—always
- स्वस्तिभिः (*svastibhiḥ*)—with blessings
- पात (*pāta*)—protect

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Vāstoṣpati, may we be united with a house that gives happiness, is delightful and is endowed with wealth. By protecting us and by bringing us what we desire, protect our excellent wealth. O gods, Vāstoṣpati and the rest, protect us always with blessings.

##### English translation
Protector of the dwelling, may we be possessed of a comfortable, delightful, opulent abode, bestowed by you : protect our wealth, whether in possession or expectation : and do you, (gods) ever cherish us with blessings.

## Sūkta 55 (Maṇḍala 7)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अमीवहेत्यष्टर्चं द्वाविंशं सूक्तं । आद्या गायत्री पंचम्याद्याश्चतस्रोनुष्टुभो द्वितीयाद्यास्तिस्र उपरिष्टाद्बृहत्यः । त्र्यृष्टुका चतुर्थ्याद्वादशके बृहतीति तल्लक्षणयोगात् । आद्या वास्तोष्पतिदेवताका शिष्टा ऐंद्र्यः । तथा चानुक्रांतं । अमीवहाष्टौ वास्तोष्पत्याद्या गायत्री शेषास्त्र्युपरिष्टाद्बृहत्यादयोनुष्टुभः प्रस्वापिन्य उपनिषदिति ॥ गतो विनियोगः ॥ बृहद्देवतायां यदुक्तं तदिह लिख्यते । वरुणस्य गृहान्प्रात्रौ वसिष्ठः स्वप्नमाचरन् । प्रविवेशाथ शं तत्र श्वा नदन्नभ्यवर्तत ॥ क्रंदंतं सारमेयं स धावंतं दष्टुमुद्यतं । यदर्जुनेति च द्वाभ्यां सांत्वयित्वा ह्यसूषुपत् । एवं प्रस्वापयामास जनमन्यं च वारुणं । बृ. दे. ६-११,१२,१३ । इति ॥ अत्र केचित्पुनरेवमाहुः । आसां प्रस्वापिनित्वं तु कथासु परिकल्प्यते । वसिष्ठस्तृषितोन्नार्थी त्रिरात्रालब्धभोजनः । चतुर्थरात्रौ चौर्यार्थं वारुणं गृहमेत्य तु । कोष्ठागारे प्रवेशाय पालकश्वादिसुप्तये । यदर्जुनेति सप्तर्चं ददर्श च जजाप चेति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The twenty-second sūkta, beginning अमीवह (*amīvaha*), has eight Ṛks. The first is Gāyatrī; the four from the fifth on are Anuṣṭubh; the three from the second on are Upariṣṭād-bṛhatī, by the definition त्र्यृष्टुका चतुर्थ्याद्वादशके बृहती (*tryṛṣṭukā caturthyādvādaśake bṛhatī*). The first is addressed to Vāstoṣpati, the rest to Indra. The Anukramaṇī says: अमीवहाष्टौ वास्तोष्पत्याद्या गायत्री शेषास्त्र्युपरिष्टाद्बृहत्यादयोनुष्टुभः प्रस्वापिन्य उपनिषदिति (*amīvahāṣṭau vāstoṣpatyādyā gāyatrī śeṣāstryupariṣṭādbṛhatyādayonuṣṭubhaḥ prasvāpinya upaniṣaditi*). The viniyoga has been given. What the Bṛhaddevatā says is written here: Vasiṣṭha, walking in sleep by night, entered Varuṇa's house; a dog came at him barking; he pacified the barking, running dog, ready to bite, with two Ṛks beginning यदर्जुन (*yadarjuna*), and put it to sleep; in the same way he put to sleep the other people of Varuṇa's household (Bṛ. De. 6-11,12,13). Some, however, say this: the power of these Ṛks to put to sleep is imagined in the tales. Vasiṣṭha, thirsty and seeking food, having got no food for three nights, on the fourth night went to Varuṇa's house to steal, to enter the storehouse and to put the guard dog and the others to sleep; he saw and muttered the seven Ṛks beginning यदर्जुन (*yadarjuna*).

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta, अमीवहा (*amīvahā*), is the twenty-second sūkta in the third anuvāka. It has eight Ṛks. The first Ṛk of this sūkta is in Gāyatrī metre. The four Ṛks from the fifth onward are in Anuṣṭubh metre;

### Printed p. 324
the three Ṛks from the second onward are in the metre called Upariṣṭād-bṛhatī. त्र्यृष्टुका चतुर्थ्याद्वादशकी बृहती (*tryṛṣṭukā caturthyādvādaśakī bṛhatī*) means that when a metre has four pādas, with eight syllables in each of the first three pādas and twelve syllables in the fourth, it is called the Upariṣṭād-bṛhatī metre. The deity of the first Ṛk of this sūkta is Vāstoṣpati; for the other Ṛks the deity is Indra. In the Anukramaṇī it is said अमीवहाष्टौ वास्तोष्पत्याद्या गायत्री शेषास्त्र्युपरिष्टाद्बृहत्यादयोनुष्टुभः प्रस्वापिन्य उपनिषत् (*amīvahāṣṭau vāstoṣpatyādyā gāyatrī śeṣāstryupariṣṭādbṛhatyādayonuṣṭubhaḥ prasvāpinya upaniṣat*). The viniyoga has been stated earlier. Concerning this sūkta, the work called Bṛhaddevatā, spoken by Śaunaka, gives this account:
वरुणस्य गृहान् रात्रौ वसिष्ठः स्वप्नमाचरन् (*varuṇasya gṛhān rātrau vasiṣṭhaḥ svapnamācaran*)
प्रविवेशाथ तं तत्र श्वा नदन्नभ्यवर्तत (*praviveśātha taṃ tatra śvā nadannabhyavartata*)
क्रंदंतं सारमेयं स धावंतं दष्टुमुद्यतं (*kraṃdaṃtaṃ sārameyaṃ sa dhāvaṃtaṃ daṣṭumudyataṃ*)
यदर्जुनेति च द्वाभ्यां सांत्वयित्वा ह्यसूसुपत् (*yadarjuneti ca dvābhyāṃ sāṃtvayitvā hyasūsupat*)
एवं प्रस्वापयामास जनमन्यं च वारुणं (*evaṃ prasvāpayāmāsa janamanyaṃ ca vāruṇaṃ*)
(Bṛ. De. 6-11,12,13.)
One night the sage Vasiṣṭha, walking in his sleep (in the state of sleep itself), went to the house of Varuṇa, his father. Then a dog that was keeping watch there came at Vasiṣṭha, barking, chasing him to bite him. Seeing the dog coming to bite him, Vasiṣṭha pacified it with two Ṛks of this sūkta beginning यदर्जुन (*yadarjuna*) (Ṛks 2-3) and made it go to sleep. Some others tell this story in another way:
आसां प्रस्वापिनित्वं तु कथासु परिकल्प्यते (*āsāṃ prasvāpinitvaṃ tu kathāsu parikalpyate*)
वसिष्ठस्तृषितोन्नार्थी त्रिरात्रालब्धभोजनः (*vasiṣṭhastṛṣitonnārthī trirātrālabdhabhojanaḥ*)
चतुर्थरात्रौ चौर्यार्थं वारुणं गृहमेत्य तु (*caturtharātrau cauryārthaṃ vāruṇaṃ gṛhametya tu*)
कोष्ठागारे प्रवेशाय पालकश्वादिसुप्तये (*koṣṭhāgāre praveśāya pālakaśvādisuptaye*)
यदर्जुनेति सप्तर्चं ददर्श च जजाप च (*yadarjuneti saptarcaṃ dadarśa ca jajāpa ca*)
(Anu. Bhā. 2-55)
Once upon a time Vasiṣṭha, having got no food for three days, weary and spent with fasting and thirst, resolved on the night of the fourth day to go to the house of his father Varuṇa and eat food, even by stealing, and entered Varuṇa's house. Then the dog that lay near the cowshed came at him to bite; Vasiṣṭha, afraid, pacified that dog with the seven Ṛks of this sūkta beginning यदर्जुन सारमेय (*yadarjuna sārameya*) (Ṛks 2-8), made it sleep, recited those Ṛks and also became the seer who beheld them as mantras. So it is said in the Anuśāsana Parva of the Mahābhārata.**NOTE**

### Printed p. 325
Maṇḍala 7; Anuvāka 3; Sūkta 55. Aṣṭaka 5; Adhyāya 4; Varga 22. Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 8.
Ṛṣi: Vasiṣṭha. Deity: 1. Vāstoṣpati; 2–8 Indra. Metre: 1. Gāyatrī; 2–4 Upariṣṭādbṛhatī; 5–8 Anuṣṭup.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒मी॒व॒हा वा॑स्तोष्पते॒ विश्वा॑ रू॒पाण्या॑वि॒शन् ।  
> सखा॑ सु॒शेव॑ एधि नः ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अमीवऽहा । वास्तोः । पते । विश्वा । रूपाणि । आऽविशन् ।  
> सखा । सुऽशेवः । एधि । नः ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे वास्तोष्पते गृहस्य पालकैतत्संज्ञक देव अमीवहामीवानां रोगाणां नाशकस्त्वं विश्वा विश्वानि बहूनि रूपाण्याविशन् प्रविशन् । यद्यद्रूपं कामयंते तत्तद्देवा विशंति । नि. १०-१७ । इति यास्कः । नोस्माकं सखा मित्रभूतः सुशेवः सुष्ठु सुखकर एधि । भव ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Vāstoṣpati, god bearing this name, protector of the house, you are the destroyer of *amīvānāṃ*, that is, of diseases. You enter all (many) forms: Yāska says (Ni. 10-17) that the gods enter whatever form they desire. Be to us a friend, one who gives good happiness.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वास्तोष्पते (*vāstoṣpate*)—O Vāstoṣpati, god
- अमीवहा (*amīvahā*)—you, destroyer of disease
- विश्वा (*viśvā*)—of many kinds
- रूपाणि (*rūpāṇi*)—forms
- आविशन् (*āviśan*)—assuming
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- सखा (*sakhā*)—a friend
- सुशेवः (*suśevaḥ*)—a giver of happiness
- एधि (*edhi*)—be

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Vāstoṣpati, destroyer of disease, assuming forms of many kinds, be to us a friend and a giver of happiness.

##### English translation
Protector of the dwelling, remover of disease, assuming all (kinds of) forms, be to us a friend the granter of happiness.

### Printed p. 326

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

To explain the derivation of the word Vāstoṣpati, Yāska has given a Nirukta on this Ṛk.
वास्तोष्पतिः । वास्तु वसतेर्निवासकर्मणस्तस्य पाता वा पालयिता वा ॥ तस्यैषा भवति ॥ अमीवहा वास्तोष्पते विश्वा रूपाण्याविशन् । सखा सुशेव एधि नः ॥ अभ्यमीवहा वास्तोष्पते सर्वाणि रूपाण्याविशन् सखा नः सुशेवो भव । शेव इति सुखनाम शिष्यतेर्वकारो नामकरणोन्तस्थान्तरोपलिंगी । विभाषितगुणः शिवमित्यप्यस्य भवति । यद्रूपं कामयते तत्तद्देवता भवति । रूपं रूपं मघवा बोभवीति इत्यपि निगमो भवति ॥ (*vāstoṣpatiḥ | vāstu vasaternivāsakarmaṇastasya pātā vā pālayitā vā ‖ tasyaiṣā bhavati ‖ amīvahā vāstoṣpate viśvā rūpāṇyāviśan | sakhā suśeva edhi naḥ ‖ abhyamīvahā vāstoṣpate sarvāṇi rūpāṇyāviśan sakhā naḥ suśevo bhava | śeva iti sukhanāma śiṣyatervakāro nāmakaraṇontasthāntaropaliṃgī | vibhāṣitaguṇaḥ śivamityapyasya bhavati | yadrūpaṃ kāmayate tattaddevatā bhavati | rūpaṃ rūpaṃ maghavā bobhavīti ityapi nigamo bhavati ‖*) (Ni. 10-17)
The word वास्तु (*vāstu*) is derived from the root वस् (*vas*) (to dwell) and means a house to live in. पतिः (*patiḥ*) means lord or protector. Hence Vāstoṣpati means the lord of the dwelling house, or its protector. To explain the use of this word, they have taken the present Ṛk as the example and given its meaning.
O lord of the house, you are the destroyer of diseases. You are able to assume many forms. Be to us always a pleasing, that is, beneficial, friend: this is the meaning of the mantra. The word शेवः (*śevaḥ*) means happiness. This word arises when the affix व (*va*) is added to the root शिष् (*śiṣ*), giving शिव (*śiva*), then guṇa comes to the शि (*śi*) and it becomes शे (*śe*); that is, the form शेव (*śeva*) is established. The word शिव (*śiva*) too is derived from the same root.
Who is this Vāstoṣpati? Some say that since this god has the power to cure diseases and ailments, Vāstoṣpati is Rudra. The gods can assume whatever forms they wish. In this matter the Vedic text रूपंरूपं मघवा बोभवीति (*rūpaṃrūpaṃ maghavā bobhavīti*) is the example.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यद॑र्जुन सारमेय द॒तः पि॑शंग॒ यच्छ॑से ।  
> वी॑व भ्राजंत ऋ॒ष्टय॒ उप॒ स्रक्वे॑षु॒ बप्स॑तो॒ नि षु स्व॑प ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यत् । अर्जुन । सारमेय । दतः । पिशंग । यच्छसे ।  
> विऽइव । भ्राजंते । ऋष्टयः । उप । स्रक्वेषु । बप्सतः । नि । सु । स्वप ॥ २ ॥

### Printed p. 327

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अर्जुन श्वेत हे सारमेय । सरमा नाम देवशुनी । तस्याः कुलोद्भव हे पिशंग केषुचिदंगेषु पिंगलवर्णैवंभूत हे शुनक त्वं दतो दंतान्यद्यदा यच्छसे विवृणोषि । अस्मान्दष्टुमित्यर्थः । तदानीं वीव भ्राजंते ऋष्टयो यथायुधानि विशेषेण भासंते तथोपास्मत्समीपे बप्सतो भक्षयतस्तव दंताः स्रक्वेषु स्रक्वासु भासंते । स्रक्वाशब्द ओष्ठप्रदेशविशेषवाचीत्यर्थः । तथाभूतस्त्वमिदानीं सुष्ठु नि स्वप । नितरां निद्रां कुरु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O white one, O Sārameya: Saramā is the name of the divine bitch, and you are born of her line. O tawny one (reddish-brown in some limbs), O dog: when you bare your teeth to bite us, then, as lances shine brilliantly, so near us your teeth, as you snap, shine in the स्रक्वेषु (*srakveṣu*); the word स्रक्वा (*srakvā*) denotes a particular part of the lips. Being such, now sleep well; sleep deeply.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अर्जुन (*arjuna*)—O white-coloured one
- पिशंग (*piśaṃga*)—and golden-hued in some limbs
- सारमेय (*sārameya*)—O Sārameya, son of Saramā
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- दतः (*dataḥ*)—teeth
- यच्छसे (*yacchase*)—you open and show, then
- वि भ्राजंते (*vi bhrājaṃte*)—shine brilliantly
- ऋष्टय इव (*ṛṣṭaya iva*)—like weapons
- उप बप्सतः (*upa bapsataḥ*)—the teeth brought near me, the mouth opened to bite
- स्रक्वेषु (*srakveṣu*)—shine along with their gums. (Even so)
- सु (*su*)—exceedingly
- नि स्वप (*ni svapa*)—close your mouth and sleep

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O white one, tawny in some limbs, son of Saramā, O Sārameya (dog): when you open your mouth to bite me and bare your teeth wide, those teeth of yours, together with the gum regions, shine like brilliantly gleaming weapons. Even so, sleep without a sound.

##### English translation
White offspring of Sarama, with tawny limbs, although barking you display your teeth against me, bristling like lances in your gums, nevertheless, go quietly to sleep,

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this Ṛk the seer Vasiṣṭha addresses as Sārameya the dog at the door of Varuṇa's house. Saramā is the divine bitch, that is, the female dog of the world of the gods. Because he is that dog's pup, he is called Sārameya. In the 108th sūkta of the tenth maṇḍala it is described how Saramā, at Indra's command, sought out the asuras called Paṇis who had stolen the cows of the gods and hidden them in a cave, conversed with them, told them the power of the gods, and so helped to bring back the stolen cows. We shall explain this matter of Saramā at length elsewhere.

### Printed p. 328
There is an earlier story about the dog described in the present Ṛk, told in the Bṛhaddevatā:
वरुणस्य गृहान्रात्रौ वसिष्ठः स्वप्न आचरत् । प्रविवेशाथ तं तत्र श्वा नदन्नभ्यधावत ॥ क्रंदंतं सारमेयं स धावंतं दष्टुमुद्यतं । यदर्जुनेति च द्वाभ्यां सांत्वयित्वा व्यसुषुपत् । स तं प्रस्वापयामास जनमन्यं च वारुणं ॥ (*varuṇasya gṛhānrātrau vasiṣṭhaḥ svapna ācarat | praviveśātha taṃ tatra śvā nadannabhyadhāvata ‖ kraṃdaṃtaṃ sārameyaṃ sa dhāvaṃtaṃ daṣṭumudyataṃ | yadarjuneti ca dvābhyāṃ sāṃtvayitvā vyasuṣupat | sa taṃ prasvāpayāmāsa janamanyaṃ ca vāruṇaṃ ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 6-13)
One night, walking in a dream, that is, asleep and without awareness, he entered the house of his father Varuṇa. At that time a dog lying there ran after him barking to bite him. To pacify it, with the Ṛks 2–8 of the present sūkta beginning यदर्जुन (*yadarjuna*) he calmed the dog and made it, and the people of Varuṇa's household lying there, fall asleep. So runs the story. The matter of calming this dog and the servants and others and making them sleep is what this sūkta describes.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स्ते॒नं रा॑य सारमेय॒ तस्क॑रं वा पुनःसर ।  
> स्तो॒तॄनिंद्र॑स्य रायसि॒ किम॒स्मान्दु॑च्छुनायसे॒ नि षु स्व॑प ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> स्तेनं । राय । सारमेय । तस्करं । वा । पुनःऽसर ।  
> स्तोतॄन् । इंद्रस्य । रायसि । किं । अस्मान् । दुच्छुनऽयसे । नि । सु । स्वप ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे पुनःसर । गतमेव देशं पुनः सरतीति पुनःसरः । तादृग्भूत हे सारमेय त्वं स्तेनं । प्रच्छन्नधनापहारी स्तेनः । तं तस्करं । प्रत्यक्षधनापहारी तस्करः । तं वा राय । गच्छ । इंद्रस्य स्तोतॄनस्मान् किं रायसि । गच्छसि । अस्मान्दुच्छुनायसे । किं बाधसे । नि षु स्वप । अत्यंतं निद्रां कुरु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O पुनःसर (*punaḥsara*), one who goes again to a place already gone to, O Sārameya: go after the स्तेन (*stena*), the one who steals wealth secretly, or the तस्कर (*taskara*), the one who takes wealth openly. Why do you go after us, the praisers of Indra? Why do you trouble us? Sleep soundly.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पुनःसर (*punaḥsara*)—you who come chasing again and again
- सारमेय (*sārameya*)—O Sārameya
- स्तेनं (*stenaṃ*)—the one who steals
- तस्करं वा (*taskaraṃ vā*)—only the thief
- राय (*rāya*)—chase after
- इंद्रस्य (*iṃdrasya*)—

### Printed p. 329
- (इंद्रस्य (*iṃdrasya*) continued) of Indra
- स्तोतॄन् (*stotṝn*)—us, the praisers
- किं रायुसि (*kiṃ rāyusi*)—why do you come chasing?
- अस्मान् (*asmān*)—us
- दुच्छुनायसे (*ducchunāyase*)—why do you trouble?
- नि सु स्वप (*ni su svapa*)—sleep without a sound

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Sārameya, who come chasing again and again, chase only the thief who steals wealth. Why do you come chasing us, the praisers of Indra? Why do you harass us? Sleep without a sound.

##### English translation
